DEC-103 (2026-10-02; no operator words recorded for the number — it follows his ruling of 2026-10-01 that every vehicle make lists its models, and he sent the prompt after "please combine with the above prompt") — THE IMPORTER'S OPTION CEILING RISES FROM 400 TO 1,500. `MAX_OPTIONS` in `src/server/imports/registry.ts` becomes 1500; it supersedes point 3 of §9 of `docs/governance/dec-050-spec.md` ("MAX_OPTIONS stays 400"), which gained a dated line. The ceiling is a bound against abuse, not an opinion about the catalogue; the 1 MB file ceiling (`MAX_BYTES`) still bounds the payload. No migration and no i18n change (the `tooManyOptions` text names no number). Rationale: catalogue batch 16 makes `model-cars` 618 options (the largest list before was 367), and the gate (`gate.ts:522`) would have refused it as `tooManyOptions`. Proof: `gate.test.ts` accepts a cell of 1,500 options and refuses 1,501, red-first with the constant still at 400. The staging timing proof asked for (`attr_split_option_cell` on a 1,500-record multibyte cell in under 2,000 ms) could not be isolated: the executor has only the REST API on staging; an 805,499-character cell returned 1,500 parts each time in whole round trips of 2,106, 2,001 and 1,357 ms, and the supervisor accepted the fastest round trip as bounding the database time. Rejected: nothing recorded. Status (2026-10-04): built and live (`5e09c8d3`, run 37038531279 green; published 2026-10-02; batch 16 imported the same afternoon against the expected "0 added · 12 changed · 0 refused"). The curator's gate is 1,500 options per list, with any list above 400 named in the change note.

DEC-104 (2026-10-02, supervisor; a harness change named as a DEC with its adoption rule fixed in advance) — THE TEST SERVICE CLIENT RETRIES A DROPPED CALL (fixes INC-385). New `e2e/helpers/net-retry.ts`, `retryingFetch(input, init)`: when fetch THROWS (no response at all) it waits and tries again, at most 3 retries after 300 ms, 1,500 ms and 5,000 ms (the waits injectable for the unit test); any Response is returned as it is, whatever its status — "A 4xx or 5xx is the server's answer and is never retried"; every method is retried, and "A replayed insert whose first copy landed must surface as the database's own duplicate error, never be swallowed"; when the retries run out it throws a TypeError naming the cause code and the attempt count; each retry appends one line to a run ledger (method, path without the query string, cause code, attempt number — never a header, a key or a body). `adminClient` (`e2e/global-setup.ts`), the only service-role client under `e2e/`, passes it as `global.fetch`; `scripts/e2e-migration-preflight.ts` also uses the service key but lies outside `e2e/` and was left as it is by ruling. `e2e/global-teardown.ts` prints "[e2e:teardown] transport retries this run: N" with counts by method and by cause code and how many ran out, even when N is 0. Unit test `src/test/e2e-net-retry.test.ts`. Rationale (stated as a hypothesis until a cause code is logged): the runtime retries a read on a closed socket by itself and does not retry a write; every one of the "fetch failed" entries in the flake ledger since 2026-09-13 is a write, and since INC-383 a failed cleanup write fails its test. Pre-committed rule (in `docs/features/e2e-harness.md`): "ADOPT after five consecutive full CI runs with no failure or flake that is a transport error in a service-client call, and with the teardown line present in every source. If any failure is traced to a replayed insert, the POST retry is removed and the rest stays." Rejected: recording the failure without a fix, which was the first disposition of the same morning and was withdrawn after the second red. Status (2026-10-05): built and landed (`5e09c8d3`); run 37038531279 was counted as the first clean run of five; no later tally and no ADOPT statement is recorded through 2026-10-05 — adoption OPEN. The teardown line is present in the newest report (run 37264070069: "transport retries this run: 0 (by method: none; by code: none; ran out: 0)" for smoke; "fetch failed" did not recur as a gating failure in the runs of 2026-10-04 → 10-05 read for this check: 37241062194, 37244952955, 37249574367, 37251884569, 37256219728, 37264070069). The executor's later local runs in this period each reported 0 transport retries.

DEC-105 (2026-10-02, operator "i guess it should be specific to category. say a realestate is in different location and we should not carry forwarn map pin, but other products/most may be in specific place so carrying forwarn makes sense. and putting location should be optional and clearly show its optional") — THE CATEGORY CAPABILITY `own_place`. A category may be marked "each listing has its own place" with the capability token `own_place`, allowed beside `bookable` and `map_pin`. A new post in such a category never carries over the pin, directions and location details of the seller's last post; every other category carries them (bundle 2 step 16). The token is enforced in two database places, both found by the executor's census: the import planner `cat_import_plan` (last declared in 8d182773, line 118; it refuses an unknown token as `badCapability`) and the table rule `categories_capabilities_check` (fd11c7ab, lines 19–23). The table rule is replaced in the bundle's one migration (DROP CONSTRAINT IF EXISTS, then ADD CONSTRAINT … CHECK (capabilities <@ ARRAY['bookable','map_pin','own_place']::text[])) and the planner is redeclared whole with the token added. The executor flagged the constraint swap as "not purely additive, so it needs your approval"; the supervisor approved it in the prompt as DEC-105 — it only widens the allowed set, every existing row stays valid and no row is changed — and the operator sent that prompt. Capabilities are set only through the categories import file; the admin category editor has no control for them. The migration changes no category row: the curator sets the token on the property leaves. Rationale: the operator's words above; a house or a plot is in a different place each time, while most other goods are sold from one place. Rejected: carrying the pin for every category (the first brief's wording, replaced the same afternoon), and the 2026-09-29 rule of never carrying it. Status (2026-10-04): built — 7423f49a (lines 889–894 and the planner) → mark `20261003000000` on ethio-prod and ethio-staging; tests CT-34 (the import accepts `own_place` and still refuses an unknown token, naming it), PW-115 and PW-116. Catalogue batch 18 set `own_place` on nine property leaves on 2026-10-03 (apartments-condos, commercial-property, condominiums, houses, land-plots, new-developments, other-real-estate, roommates-shared, short-term-rentals; not realtor-services, which is the agent's office), replacing `map_pin` there; nothing reads `map_pin` since the change of 2026-09-30 that offers the map on every category. The operator's final bundle 2 walk (next block) confirmed that a new ad under Real Estate › Houses carries no pin.

DECISIONS OF THIS PERIOD THAT RECEIVED THEIR NUMBERS IN THE NEXT ONE. The eight decisions below were made by the operator on 2026-10-02 and 2026-10-03 and saved the same day in the project memory, with the note that they would enter the repository ledger "with the next brief Lovable receives after bundle 2, as three decisions that amend REQ-007 and REQ-008: contact visibility, alias rules, and the store page and address". The numbers DEC-106 to DEC-113 were assigned on 2026-10-03 in the supervisor's running record; the full paragraphs, with what was built, are in the next session block. What was decided here, and in what words:
- Contact visibility (later DEC-106; amends REQ-007). On 2026-10-02 at 19:51 the operator, told of INC-389, asked for research before any decision: "need to do a detailed reaserch on this firt and need to discuss first" — whether a seller who elects to show a phone or e-mail should be visible only to logged-in users, what the industry does against data miners "and what explicit reasurance we can give for each". The supervisor's research (OLX Poland shows numbers only to signed-in users since 2021; Avito substitutes forwarding numbers; Kijiji relays e-mail; a scraper for Jiji's country sites is sold online; OWASP: no single control stops harvesting) came with a per-channel proposal. His answers at 20:12: "1. Direct channels for signed-in buyers only, with no public phone option for now? yes agree"; "2. No email channel? yes". Agreed with them: phone, second phone and WhatsApp are shown to signed-in buyers who tap to show them, the Telegram handle likewise; messages on ethio.com are for signed-in buyers ("Buyers write to you here. They never see your number."); e-mail is not offered as a channel, because messages reach the seller by e-mail notification and the address is never shown; the seller's switch stays and reads "Show to signed-in buyers"; every view is recorded and one account can open only a limited number per day; a public phone option is to be revisited when verified businesses exist. Stated limits: some buyers leave at a sign-in wall; many accounts can still collect numbers slowly; the consent wording needs a legal check under Ethiopia's Personal Data Protection Proclamation 1321/2024 before launch.
- Public links and sharing (part of the later DEC-108). His question 3 answer and his clarification at 20:25 — "most users who post in instagram are not businesses, or dont want to open busness accounts so restricting may prevent them. so are we talking about same thing or something different?" — separated two things. Sharing OUT (the seller posts the ad to their own Telegram, WhatsApp, Facebook or Instagram with a link back; ACT-G1) stays for every seller. Links IN (a website, Telegram channel or social page shown on the seller's page) belong to the optional store page and may be added by any seller who opens one, person or business; a link to a personal chat, or a WhatsApp link, is a contact detail and stays behind sign-in; every link is screened when saved. At 20:33: "on Sharing out and links in - agree with your decision earlier". Not settled: how to tell a Telegram channel from a personal account when a link is entered (left to the storefront spec).
- Seller-name rules (later DEC-107; amends REQ-008). His opening at 19:51: "currently our system suggests a numbers and we should i think avoid giveing aliase like 12345", with the wish to protect "important names including category brand names, main attributes we use, or mmajor brands impersonation" and the questions whether names come from the first and last name or the business name, whether frequent replacement is allowed, and whether a changed name stays traceable. His answers: to the brand rule, "yes, but I dont want a user to use ethio anywhere even if its ethio_coffee, etc. that may be confusing for users who see it"; to how long a previous name is shown, after asking for the risks of one year against indefinitely, "i guess 1 year is ok, I was talking about aliases not the web address"; "Does the "ethio" rule also cover the business name buyers see, including the Amharic spelling? - yes"; and again at 20:45, "same aliases cant take ethio name." The rule set as agreed and saved that evening: 5 to 30 characters, Latin letters, digits and underscore, starting with a letter, at least three letters, no run of seven or more digits, no double or edge underscores (the code then allowed 3 to 30 characters, so "12345" and a phone number passed); three suggestions to tap, built from the business name or the first and last name, never from the e-mail, with at most two digits added when a name is taken, preferring names without an underscore, an Amharic name being converted to Latin letters by a fixed table (this last point was changed on 2026-10-03, next block); any name containing "ethio" is refused, including lookalike spellings and letters split by underscores — read literally this also refuses "ethiopia" and "ethiopian", and ኢትዮጵያ in a business name, which the supervisor stated twice and the operator did not change; platform words (admin, support, official, verified, moderator, staff) are refused as whole words or at the start or end of a name, not inside another word; category, attribute, place and country names are refused as a whole name and allowed inside a longer one; brands are refused on exact match against the catalogue's brand lists (640 names then) plus a seeded list of banks, telecoms, airlines and government bodies, a brand beside only a claim word is refused ("samsung_official"), a brand beside the seller's own name is allowed ("selam_samsung"), lookalikes are folded before comparing (0/o, 1/l, rn/m); e-mail provider names are protected and a country or domain ending is refused only after a protected name ("telebirr_et" refused, "abebe_et" allowed); the AI imitation check stays as a second layer and a name it cannot judge is accepted and queued for review; one change per 30 days with one free correction within 24 hours, checking a name no longer claims it, an old name stays bound to its account and is never given to another seller; the previous name is shown in public for one year, while the full name history, the forwarding of the old address and "Member since" are permanent and an admin can hide a previous name early in a safety case; the refusal message must say why and be followed by three suggestions. Of these, the operator answered in his own words the brand rule, the "ethio" rule, the business-name extension and the one-year display; the rest are the supervisor's proposals that he covered with "I agree with the above … lets save it as agreed rule" and "save the above". On 2026-10-03 at 12:45 he asked what happens when a reserved organisation ("for example Ethiopian Airlines") comes to open a shop; the answer — the list reserves the name for its real owner and does not ban it: a request from the refusal message, verification per REQ-008 (an e-mail from the official domain, documents, a call back on the publicly listed number), assignment by an admin on a path that the reserved list never blocks and that records who approved it and on what evidence, then the verified badge — he accepted with "ok, sounds good."
- Store page and address (later DEC-108; amends REQ-008). "store page for everyone - yes optional if they want" (20:25): any seller, person or business, may open one; the alias and the store address are one name, and when the alias changes the old address forwards to the new one. On the address he worried that "adding @confuses with email address", was against `ethio.com/shop/name` ("its against making the characters short"), asked for "a deeper more reaserch and workaround" for `ethio.com/abebe`, and named the fallback, "the other option is the original @". At 20:45, on the root address with its safeguards: "I agree with the above but want to know if its going to affect usability, performance etc? if it doesnt lets save it as agreed rule then." The supervisor's review found no performance cost that matters and small usability costs, and at 20:47 he wrote "save the above". Agreed: the store address is `ethio.com/name` at the root, replacing the July decision `ethio.com/@name`; seller names are kept apart from site pages by shape (every page at the root is shorter than 5 characters, or contains a hyphen, or is on a reserved list seeded with a few hundred common site words that an admin can extend); the build fails if a root page is added that could also be a seller name and is not on the list, and a nightly check confirms no seller holds a reserved word; the site's own page wins a clash; function words (login, verify, password, payment and similar) are refused in a seller name; category-and-city lists stay under `/c/`. Lasting cost stated: a later single-word page whose word a seller already holds is named with a hyphen instead.
- The price page owns the deal (later DEC-109). At 21:37 the operator asked for an analysis: the unit of sale, amount and quantity "are more related to the price itself and may be part of the price list part of wizard", to be moved "without loosing all what we worked its relationship with the different attributes". The supervisor's census (27 service categories with a pricing basis, 38 goods categories with a unit of sale, 86 others; nothing on Specifications depends on the questions to be moved) recommended it. His answers at 21:57: "1. Move the unit of sale, size of one unit, pieces per pack and quantity to the price page? yes". On payment terms and minimum hire he asked for an explanation, adding "I think payment terms are related to price"; the supervisor's test — "does the answer describe the thing, or the deal? The thing stays on Specifications; the deal goes on the price page" — moves the real-estate minimum term, payment frequency and payment plan and the hire minimum to the price page, and adds a period question (per day, week, month or year) when the offer is rent, lease or hire, a gap on 18 categories where a rental showed a bare amount. At 22:05: "For you to confirm: on rentals and hires, should the price page ask the period (per day, week, month, year)? yes agree with all you mentioned, proceed." The offer type (sale, rent, lease), condition, warranty, delivery, exchange and "included" items stay on Specifications; on four beauty categories with no unit of sale the volume stays there too.
- Title and description after the price (later DEC-110): "2. Put title and description after price? yes" (his reason at 21:37: "It may give context for the ai").
- The price is never written into the title or the description (later DEC-111): "4. Keep the price amount out of the title and description? yes" (at 21:37 he had doubted Jiji's habit: "not sure if that is a good idea"). The writing helper may mention the unit and "negotiable".
- The default image and an optional "Photos coming soon" ribbon (later DEC-112), 21:57: "if users dont upload their own picture in image page, an option to add a ribbon saying images coming soon as option and show it in the default image- see exact apec implemnentation for it and placement should be nice and exact as the background ethio.com diagonal watermark so it matches but the color as in apex, size and needs to fit. when user posts image remove that. optional click." and "in previous i dont see the default image when users dont post image, please make sure it shows in final step for edit and preview." ("Apex" is a reference repository, `github.com/tesfayekb/apex-marketplace`, whose ribbon component `src/features/listings/components/ComingSoonRibbon.tsx` the supervisor read.) Agreed plan: a text band so it translates, in the main green, across the bottom-right corner at −30° to match the watermark, sized as a share of the image, offered only while the ad uses the default image, gone when a real photo is added; one shared picture component so the default image and the ribbon look the same on every screen (the review step's small card printed "No photo yet").
- Finished steps clickable on big screens (later DEC-113), 21:57: "on big screen the left side items showing the steps we are in, as well in big screens the top once, can we click a specific step and return back to that step". Agreed: the left list and the top bar become clickable like the phone strip; any visited step opens directly and steps not yet reached stay locked, because the server checks them in order; a category change already resets specifications, title, description, video link and price with a ten-second Undo.
The supervisor recorded all five wizard items for "the wizard bundle", to be briefed once bundle 2 and the contact-permissions fix were closed, "so Lovable is not handed new work mid-task".

VISIBILITY PLAN, ACT-G1 TO ACT-G4 AND SEARCH CONSOLE (2026-10-02).
- Origin: at 07:54 the operator turned the morning's sharing discussion into a task and asked for more: "ok, lets make that as future task and document in repo and also i want you to review and comeup with any other idea that will improve our visibility, ofcourse will work on CEO of the website, in future(tell me if best to do it now and what we need to change while we are building categories search items etc. also any other way to improve our visibility." ("CEO" is SEO.) The supervisor's answer: search-engine work needs no project of its own now, but a few rules are cheap to build in and costly to retrofit. `docs/governance/visibility-plan.md` was written through the executor at `8fa761d4`: "Nothing here is built or scheduled. Every item needs its own approved spec before a build. Candidates are ideas, not decisions."
- Its §1, sharing and our own channels (operator decision): sellers share their own listing through Telegram, WhatsApp, Facebook and copy-link buttons at the end of posting and on every public listing page; ethio.com posts automatically only on accounts it owns (its own Telegram channels by category and city, fed by a bot with every approved listing, which marks or removes its post when the listing sells or is taken down); never posting into anyone else's account, channel or group, and never publishing lists of other people's groups; a shared link shows a preview card (photo, title, price, city); only listings that passed screening (REQ-021) are shareable; later, after Meta's app review, posting to a seller's own Facebook Page or Instagram business account; candidate: a seller forwards a post from their own Telegram channel to our bot and it becomes a draft listing.
- Its §2, search-engine rules R1–R9. Decided now: R1, category slugs and option values never change once public (a change needs a redirect); R2, no hand-written search text per category — titles and descriptions come from templates filled from data, and the catalogue aliases are the search synonyms; R3, the listing title is built from the seller's answers; R4, the photo step saves one share-size image per listing and uses the listing title as each picture's text alternative. For the browse-phase (U7) spec: R5, the place is in the URL (category × city pages), a listing URL carries a permanent id plus readable words, each page names one preferred address; R6, lists, listing pages and shop pages are rendered on the server with real titles, price, make and model are marked up as structured data, and a sitemap is generated; R7, only make, model and brand filter pages are indexable; R8, a sold or removed listing keeps its page for a period and shows similar listings. For launch: R9, old ethio.com addresses redirect to their new homes, the home page and `?lang=am` carry over, gambling-spam and test pages answer "gone", and the executor platform's published address names ethio.com as the preferred address.
- Its §4, seven candidates, not decided: price pages built from our own listings; bulk upload for dealers; saved-search alerts; shop pages with a printable QR code; share results shown to the seller; a Play Store listing; invite e-mails to old ethio.com users (DEC-003).
- Tracker lines, appended to `docs/tracking/action-tracker.md` under a new prefix the supervisor first checked was free: "ACT-G1 — Share buttons and link previews (visibility-plan §1). FUTURE; rides the public listing page (U7)."; "ACT-G2 — ethio.com's own Telegram channels fed by a bot (visibility-plan §1). FUTURE."; "ACT-G3 — Search-engine rules R5–R8 enter the U7 spec; R9 enters the launch gate (visibility-plan §2)."; "ACT-G4 — The listing title built from the seller's answers joins the wizard's remaining work (R3)." Status (2026-10-04): ACT-G1, ACT-G2 and ACT-G3 open, nothing built or specified; for ACT-G4 the title built from the seller's answers was built in bundle 4 Part C (`buildTitle`, changelog 2026-10-04), and the tracker line itself is unchanged.
- Search Console: on 2026-10-02 at 08:21 the operator reported ethio.com's ownership verified ("Verification method: Domain name provider" — a DNS record in his Cloudflare zone that must stay) and sent the Performance export. It holds 2025-05-30 to 2025-06-18 only (20 days): 306 clicks and 25,230 impressions; 76% of clicks from Ethiopia, 14% from the United States, 87% on phones; 219 of the 306 clicks landed on the home page and the searches were the site's name; six business pages drew 55 clicks and 19,196 impressions; the 256 category pages drew 16 clicks. Four gambling-spam pages (1xBet, 1win and two casino pages) were on the old WordPress site; at 08:35 the operator reported: "on wordpress done, changed that user to pending vendor, so cant post again, used to be  developer. 4. left menu no issues detected. remind me in 3 days". A reminder for the second export was set for Monday 2026-10-05 08:36 ET. Consequences recorded: shop pages move up (business type and city in the title; the six businesses first to be invited back); Amharic category titles; the redirect rules of R9; no sitemap is submitted until the browse phase.

CLASS RULES FORGED OR RESTATED (supervisor practice from here on; none was proposed as a numbered amendment to the supervisor instructions or to the executor's Project Knowledge, and nothing was installed by the operator):
- Every multi-turn brief opens with STEP 0: save the brief unchanged under `docs/governance/briefs/`, name the path in `roadmap.md`, read it first on every turn (INC-388).
- No prompt is produced for an agent that is mid-task; finished work is verified first, then one prompt per agent with everything folded in (operator directive).
- The record is searched before the operator is asked whether something was done; when the record is silent, the step is written so that it answers itself (operator directive).
- A whole redeclaration keeps every header attribute of the live function, and the pre-apply comparison reads `provolatile`, `prosecdef` and `proconfig` beside the argument list (INC-391). A function fix never drops and re-creates unless the argument list really changes. A migration's mark is chosen at apply time (INC-390). A corrective's proofs use RAISE EXCEPTION, not ASSERT, and check that `plpgsql.check_asserts` is not off.
- A cleanup helper throws on a failed delete; a scratch root sorts at 2,000,000 or higher; no box is read while an animation runs (INC-383, INC-384).
- A pre-committed adoption rule accompanies every harness change (DEC-104); only a thrown fetch is retried, never a server's answer.
- After changing a screen, every spec that exercises it is run, not only the new tests. The end-of-bundle run is the brief's list of specs, run in parts that finish inside one turn, at 2 workers; the full-suite proof is CI on the final commit, which the supervisor reads.
- A browser test types and measures as a person would; a walk finding outranks a green test (INC-394).
- The executor runs its browser tests once per turn, at the end; red-first runs are done together, by restoring the old screen for one run or against a copy of the app from before the brief.
- Operator bandwidth: an interim executor report is brought to the supervisor only if it asks for a ruling, reports a failure, or is the final bundle report; the wording for the operator's reply is "Continue. Do not stop after one step: work through as many steps as your turn allows, and stop only when the turn is about to end."
- Walk lines use categories verified to show the behaviour being checked.
- Product research runs research → discussion → operator decision → saved in the project memory → entered in the repository ledger with the next brief; nothing goes to the executor while it is mid-task.
- Curator: removing an option goes links first, then definitions, as two confirms; the gates are 1,500 options per list (any list above 400 named in the change note) and 150 values per allowed list; an option holds at most five finder words.

SCOPE AND TIME. This block covers the operator's afternoon and evening of Saturday 2026-10-03, 15:19 to 23:28 America/New_York (ET, UTC−4), which is 2026-10-03 19:19 to 2026-10-04 03:28 UTC. Times below are UTC unless marked ET; the repository's commit and CI stamps are UTC, so work done after 20:00 ET carries the date 2026-10-04. The supervisor's working notes are also stamped in UTC, but by hand and only to within a few minutes. A "bundle" is one brief handed to the executor that covers several items, is saved in the repository under `docs/governance/briefs/`, and closes on a green CI run for its final commit plus one operator walk on the published site. Bundle 2 was the "place and contact" bundle of the posting wizard (`docs/governance/briefs/bundle-2.md`). Bundle 3 is the subject of this block (`docs/governance/briefs/bundle-3.md`). During the period the later bundles were renumbered: bundle 4 is the posting-wizard bundle (step order, price page, title, picture, lifetime, and the four catalogue-engine items the curator waits for) and bundle 5 is the close-out bundle (speed checks, the permission-function fix and the older database warnings, CI housekeeping and the test-account pool, the records turn and one final full test run). Earlier lists that call the engine items "bundle 3" and the close-out "bundle 4" are superseded. "C27" is the curator's catalogue cycle 27, delivered in numbered batches. "M1", "M1b", "M2", "M3" and "M4" are the migrations of bundle 3 in the order they were written; a "mark" is the row a migration writes into `public.migration_marks` as its last statement, and "apply <fragment> → expect mark <value>" is the line with which the executor hands a migration to the operator for ethio-staging. The decision and incident ledgers were deliberately not written during bundles 3 and 4 (each brief says so); the numbers in this block were held in the supervisor's running record until this import.

PROCESS CHANGE BEFORE BUNDLE 3. At 16:06 ET the operator asked that the executor be given several fixes at a time, because "at times completing running a test for single turn takes more than 1 hr and delays completion", and at 16:09 ET made clear he did not want fewer tests. The supervisor measured where the time went: the executor's own full local run of the suite (1,022 tests at the time) took about two hours on one machine inside its turn; CI runs every test on every push on six shards in about 22 minutes and main moves only on a fully green run; the nightly runs everything one at a time, including the tests that change shared data. The answer was DEC-114 and DEC-115 (below), approved by the operator at 16:13 ET, and later DEC-119. From bundle 3 on: larger bundles, fewer of them; the executor tests once per turn, not once per step; the operator sends only "continue" and brings the supervisor only a ruling the executor asks for, a CI red that is still red after the executor's next turn, or the final report; the brief is delivered as an attached file with a one-line paste, and the operator's paste is the approval of the brief. The stated trade-off: one longer walk at the end instead of several short ones, and a defect found late touches more work.

CLOSE-OUT REVIEW OF BUNDLE 3 (four lenses, recorded at 9ba4ff73). Security: contact details, exact pins and the catalogue are closed to outsiders; limits are enforced in the database; reserved names and "ethio" are refused; two items logged for later (INC-409 the permission function, INC-410 the option-list limit). Functionality: everything in the brief is built and tested; the executor's final report mislabels the first migration. Performance: the phone library loads only on the contact step (about 29 KB, once); nothing was added to the first page load. Usability: decided by the operator's walk (below).

DEC-106 (2026-10-02, operator "yes agree") — CONTACT DETAILS AND EXACT PINS ARE FOR SIGNED-IN BUYERS ONLY. A listing's direct contact channels (phone, second phone, WhatsApp, Telegram) and its exact map pin are private: no visitor and no other user can read them through the data API. A signed-in buyer sees them only by asking ("Show contact"), receives only the channels the seller switched on, and each first look at a listing is recorded and counted against a daily limit per account (dial `contact_reveal`, 30 per day); a second look at the same listing on the same day is not counted and the owner is never counted. The seller's switch reads "Show to signed-in buyers". There is no public phone option and no email channel for now. "Messages on ethio.com" is the default channel, always on, and cannot be switched off. An approximate pin is blurred on the server (rounded to two decimals) and the area drawn around it has a radius of 800 m, so the real spot always lies inside it. The decision amends REQ-007. Rationale: the operator asked on 2026-10-02 for research before deciding; it found that phone numbers on classifieds are harvested (a scraper for Jiji's country sites is sold online) and that peers moved to signed-in or relayed contact, and INC-389 had shown that the columns were readable by anonymous callers. Rejected: a public phone option; an email channel. Status (2026-10-04): built in bundle 3 steps 7 and 10 — M1 b9aa66a4 → mark 20261003220000 and M1b 2a467fcc → mark 20261003223000 on ethio-prod and ethio-staging; screens of Part B; tests PR-21, PW-12; on main at 9ba4ff73. Not built: the buyer-facing "Show contact" button (the door `reveal_listing_contact` is built and waits for the public ad page), and messaging itself, on which the line "We email you when a message arrives" depends.

DEC-107 (2026-10-02, operator "yes, but I dont want a user to use ethio anywhere even if its ethio_coffee"; one detail changed 2026-10-03, operator "yes") — THE SELLER-NAME RULES. The seller name (alias) is the public name of a seller and the future address of the seller's page. Shape: 5 to 30 characters of a–z, 0–9 and underscore, starting with a letter, with at least three letters, no run of seven or more digits, no underscore at either end and no two together. Every comparison is made on a fold that is never stored: lower case; underscores removed; 0→o, 1→l, 3→e, 4→a, 5→s; then rn→m; for Amharic, spaces and punctuation removed and same-sound letters equal. The rules run in this order and the first that matches refuses, each with its own message in English and Amharic: (a) shape; (b) the folded name contains "ethio" — this also refuses a business name containing "ethio" or «ኢትዮ»; (c) a role word (admin, administrator, support, official, verified, moderator, staff) or a function word (login, logout, signin, signup, verify, password, payment) as a whole part or at the start or end, so that "badminton_shop" stays allowed; (d) exact match with a site word, a category slug or English category name, the English name of any place or country, a catalogue brand name, or a protected handle; (e) a protected name beside only claim words (store, shop, official, agent, a country code and the like); (f) taken — another account holds the name now or held it before. The business name, which is what buyers see for a business, is judged by rules b, d and e (operator "yes", 2026-10-03). Checking a name does not claim it; it is claimed when the contact step is saved. Every name an account has held is kept and is never issued to another account; the first name is free; afterwards one change in 30 days, with one correction within 24 hours that does not count; for 365 days after a change the seller line shows "previously <old name>", and it always shows "Member since <month year>". Three suggestions follow every refusal, each already checked as free, built from the business name or the first and last name, never from the email or the display name, preferring forms without an underscore and adding at most two digits. An admin may assign a reserved name, with a recorded reason of at least ten characters and the audit row 'user.alias_assigned'. The AI imitation check stays as a second layer; when the AI is unavailable the name is accepted and 'alias.unchecked' is audited. Protected lists: 331 site, role and function words; the curator's 435 reserved names with 2,162 folded handles; 78 exact-only words; the catalogue's brand and make options (633 folded names in 40 lists, read live). Changed on 2026-10-03: suggestions are made only from a name typed in Latin letters; an Amharic name gets no suggestion and a hint asks for Latin letters. Rationale: impersonation of banks, telecoms, public bodies and of the site itself; the operator did not want numeric names such as "12345" or the word "ethio" in any seller's name because it would confuse buyers; the name is also the store address (DEC-108). Rejected: allowing "ethio" inside a longer name such as "ethio_coffee" (the supervisor's first proposal, overruled by the operator); transliterating Amharic names by a fixed table for suggestions (it writes ትግስት as "tigst"). On how long the previous name is shown the operator said "i guess 1 year is ok"; the record of it is kept permanently. Status (2026-10-04): built — M2 18556a32 → marks 20261004010309 and 20261004020000 on ethio-prod and ethio-staging; screens and routes of steps 18–22; tests `alias-rules.test.ts`, PW-130, PW-131, AU-12; on main at 9ba4ff73. Amended after the walk by M4, M4b and M4c (INC-412: a second reading with edge digits removed; suggestions also use the category; a line under the box says when the next change is allowed), on main at b432691b. Later changes to the contact step belong to bundle 4.

DEC-108 (2026-10-02, operator "I agree with the above but want to know if its going to affect usability, performance etc? if it doesnt lets save it as agreed rule then. same aliases cant take ethio name.") — THE STORE ADDRESS IS ethio.com/<name> AT THE ROOT. A seller's optional store page will live at ethio.com/<seller name>, replacing the July decision ethio.com/@name in REQ-008; the seller name is the address, so one name serves both. The store page is optional and open to any seller, person or business; public links (website, channel, social page) attach to it for any seller who opens one, each link screened, while a personal chat or WhatsApp link stays a contact detail behind sign-in. Safeguards, so that no site page can be mistaken for a seller: seller names have the DEC-107 shape (five characters or more, no hyphen); every first path segment of the site's own routes is shorter than five characters, or contains a hyphen, or is on the reserved site-word list; an automatic check fails the build otherwise; the site's own pages win; category and place lists stay under /c/; a daily job counts accounts whose name the rules refuse today. Rationale: the operator wanted the simplest, shortest address; "@" reads like an email address (ethio.com/@gmail.com) and /shop/ makes the address longer. Rejected: ethio.com/shop/<name>; ethio.com/@<name>. The supervisor's usability and performance review, saved at the operator's request, found no performance concern (a seller page is one indexed lookup by name; addresses that are not name-shaped are rejected by shape). Status (2026-10-04): the guard is built in bundle 3 step 23 — `scripts/site-words.txt`, `scripts/check-root-routes.ts` with its self-test in the "Build, typecheck, lint" job, and the pg_cron job 'seller-name-sweep' writing `seller_name_sweep_runs` (in M2); on main at 9ba4ff73. Not built: the store page route, public links, the verified badge and the admin path that gives a protected organisation its own business name (it comes with verified businesses).

DEC-109 (2026-10-02, operator "yes agree with all you mentioned, proceed") — THE PRICE PAGE OWNS THE DEAL. The unit of sale, the size of one unit (net weight, volume), pieces per pack and the quantity available move from the specifications page to the price page of the posting wizard, beside the price and the negotiable switch; when the offer is rent, lease or hire the price page asks the price period; the minimum term (a number with its own unit) and the payment terms are asked there too. No relationship already built between these rows and the other attributes may be lost. Rationale, in the operator's reasoning of 2026-10-02: the unit of sale, the quantity and the size of one unit are more related to the price than to the specifications, and hire and rental periods and payment terms are all related to price. It reverses the placement of 2026-09-30 (the unit asked on the specifications page, item N2), which the bundle 4 brief retires. Status at the end of this period: decided, not built, queued for bundle 4. Status (2026-10-04): built in bundle 4 Part B — M5 20261004144146_923dd4cb → mark 20261004090000 (`deal_group`, `deal_keys`, `price_basis_in_force`, `listings.price_unit`) and the price-page screens, tests PW-136 to PW-139; on main at fcc9b822, run 37241062194; walked by the operator on 2026-10-05 after the Publish of turn 10 (walk line 2: the price page's unit, Serves and the built title; line 3: a unit held by the type — "OK"; the walk's defects were elsewhere: INC-434, INC-435, INC-436). The rent and hire period rows themselves are catalogue rows owed by the curator after bundle 4 (the engine batch, not yet sent).

DEC-110 (2026-10-02, same approval) — TITLE AND DESCRIPTION COME AFTER THE PRICE. The order a seller walks becomes category → specifications → photos → price → title and description → place → contact → review. Rationale: the operator's reason was that the price step before them may give context to the AI writing helper. Status at the end of this period: decided, not built. Status (2026-10-04): built in bundle 4 Part A (M5; the door's step numbers 4 = price and 5 = title, DEC-120); on main at fcc9b822; not yet walked.

DEC-111 (2026-10-02, same approval) — THE PRICE IS NEVER WRITTEN INTO THE TITLE OR THE DESCRIPTION. The title is built from the seller's answers; the writing helper sees the unit and the size but is never sent the amount or the currency. Rationale: the operator doubted that the habit, seen on Jiji, of putting the price into the title or description is a good one. Status at the end of this period: decided, not built. Status (2026-10-04): built in bundle 4 Part C (`src/features/posting/build-title.ts`, the helper's prompt, PW-140); on main at fcc9b822; not yet walked.

DEC-112 (2026-10-02, operator agreed to proceed) — THE DEFAULT IMAGE, AND AN OPTIONAL "PHOTOS COMING SOON" RIBBON. When a seller uploads no photo the category's default image is shown, including on the last step's edit and preview where the operator had not seen it; an optional tick adds a ribbon "Photos coming soon" across that image, following the Apex implementation the operator named, placed to match the diagonal ethio.com watermark, coloured as in Apex, sized to fit, and removed when the seller posts a photo. Status at the end of this period: decided, not built. Status (2026-10-05): built in bundle 4 Part D (`src/components/marketplace/listing-picture.tsx`, `listings.photos_soon`, PW-141); walked 2026-10-05 (line 6): the default picture OK; the ribbon failed the walk — drawn as a 150 %-wide band across the whole 4:3 box instead of a corner band on the picture (INC-435) — and was rebuilt in turn 11 (`70e16ea5`): a frame sized to the picture's own ratio, a corner band at the lower right at −45°, in `bg-primary` / `text-primary-foreground` (the operator ruled out yellow and the watermark colour), `data-testid listing-photos-soon-ribbon` kept, PW-141 measures the frame; walked again "looks good".

DEC-113 (2026-10-02, operator agreed to proceed) — FINISHED STEPS ARE CLICKABLE ON BIG SCREENS. On wide screens the step list at the left and the top bar let the seller return to a specific step without pressing Back repeatedly; as built, a step not yet reached stays plain text. Status at the end of this period: decided, not built. Status (2026-10-05): built in bundle 4 step 5 (PW-135, at 1280); on main at fcc9b822; walk line 6 of 2026-10-05 asked for it ("At 1280 px wide: finished steps in the left list are clickable") and the recorded result of line 6 names only the picture and the ribbon.

DEC-114 (2026-10-03, operator "agree with your recommendations. yes next step. make sure everything is secure and doesnt cause further issues.") — THE EXECUTOR RUNS ONLY THE TOUCHED SPECS LOCALLY; CI ON THE FINAL COMMIT IS THE FULL-SUITE PROOF. The executor never runs the whole E2E suite inside its turn. Its local run covers the tests for the files it changed; CI keeps running every test on every push, and nothing reaches main until CI is fully green; a new test must still fail on the old screen first, and those red runs are done together. Rationale: the operator's directive of 2026-10-03 that a single turn's test run was taking more than an hour, with his condition "I am not saying we have to do fewer tests … I dont want to make it less secure less comprehensive in terms of testing"; the delay came from the executor repeating inside its turn (about two hours) what CI does in about 22 minutes. Rejected: moving more tests to the nightly — the largest candidate, the admin console tests (about a third of all test time), would save only a few minutes per commit on six shards, and a break there could sit unnoticed for up to a day. Status (2026-10-04): in force since the bundle 3 brief; tightened by DEC-119.

DEC-115 (2026-10-03, same approval) — A SCRIPT CHOOSES THE LOCAL TESTS, UNDER A DECISION RULE FIXED IN ADVANCE. `scripts/e2e-select.ts` prints the E2E spec files a change must run: one table maps source areas to spec globs; a changed spec selects itself; a changed E2E helper selects every spec that imports it; a migration selects the areas named on its `-- e2e-areas:` header line and ALL without one; an unmapped file under `src/` or a harness file (`playwright.config.ts`, the global setup and teardown, `scripts/serve-e2e-node.ts`) selects ALL. `bun run e2e:changed` runs the list locally; a self-test in the build job fails when a spec is reachable from no area or an area's glob matches nothing. No CI test job, shard or lane changes. A decision was required because the E2E harness is a closed surface (G22). Decision rule, recorded before any result: over the next ten CI runs that follow a local selector run, count the CI failures in specs the selector did not print; 0 or 1, keep the script; 2 or more, widen the table or return to naming the specs in each brief. Rationale: the choice of local tests had been left to the executor's judgment, and on the morning of 2026-10-03 it changed the place step without running the older place-step tests. Status (2026-10-04): built in bundle 3 Part 0 (39f19b2b; `docs/features/e2e-harness.md`, "Choosing specs by what changed"). The rule is still running: no tally of the ten runs is recorded in the repository, the supervisor's notes or the transcript, and DEC-119 limited the selector's local use to lists of five files or fewer.

DEC-116 (2026-10-03, operator "I dont want cost to rise while we are still building, but wants to make it difficult to access for outside users. if the cost raises that much now … then best to hold on that until launch.") — THE REPOSITORY STAYS PUBLIC WHILE BUILDING AND GOES PRIVATE AT LAUNCH. Rationale: on a private repository GitHub Actions is billed; the supervisor's ESTIMATE was about 160 billable minutes per completed run at $0.006, about $0.96 a run, and 36 completed runs in three days, so $300–600 a month at the current pace. A second reason not to switch now: the supervisor's session reads the repository only as a public visitor — its GitHub connection is signed in to the operator's account and is not rate limited, but the repository is not attached to the session, and the session has no tool to attach it — so a switch to private would end all reading of code and CI. Consequences: a launch-gate item — attach the repository to the supervisor's session first, test, and only then switch; the CI cost plan (GitHub's runners or a runner of our own, which is a harness change needing its own decision under G22) is decided then; the data lock that costs nothing was built at once (INC-397); and, because the repository is public, detailed notes on an open security hole enter it only after the fix is live. Rejected: the supervisor's recommendation to switch to private before the bundle 3 brief was saved. Status (2026-10-05): decided; the launch-gate line is not yet in `docs/governance/launch-gate.md` (dev `48d3c53b`). On 2026-10-04 (13:54 and 14:05 local) the operator asked whether anyone copies the public repository; the standing decision was kept (block S51: public while building, private at launch, access test first; ESTIMATE of going private now 300–600 US dollars a month).

DEC-117 (2026-10-03, operator "active until should for now be indefinate and no date but user can change it to specific date and may need to have a control on admin side how long we give the publication time to be posted depending the level or type of advertizment- need to buld that") — LISTING LIFETIME: NO END DATE BY DEFAULT. An ad has no end unless the seller sets a date or its category holds a limit; the admin sets the limit by category now and by ad level (plan) later. Rationale: the operator's priority, stated on 2026-09-28, is to get many products posted, so a listing stays until the seller removes it. Known cost: ads for sold items stay up until the seller removes them; a "still available?" reminder and notices before an ad expires can cover that later. Status at the end of this period: decided, not built (with INC-400). Status (2026-10-04): built in bundle 4 step 16 — M5 923dd4cb → mark 20261004090000; test PR-23; the review step's "Stays up until you take it down." and the admin field "Listing lifetime (days)"; on main at fcc9b822; no walk line for the lifetime is recorded (the 2026-10-05 walk list had none). Lifetime by plan level waits for the posting-limits bundle ("admin posting limits and AI switches" in the operator-facing list of 2026-10-04).

DEC-118 (2026-10-03) — PHONE NUMBERS ARE READ BY libphonenumber-js/min 1.11.18, LOADED ON DEMAND. The contact step reads every typed number with `parsePhoneNumberFromString` from `libphonenumber-js/min`, pinned at exactly 1.11.18 (the version `phone-plans.ts` was taken from), loaded by a dynamic import when the contact step opens, never imported statically, and listed in FORBIDDEN in `scripts/check-marketplace-imports.mjs` so the first-paint budget cannot regress. The supervisor measured a parse-only build at 28.8 KB gzip ("about 29 KB"). It is the first dependency of its kind, so Knowledge rule A7 applied (the installed package's exports and a smoke proof were pasted). If the library cannot be loaded the step says so and offers a retry; it never falls back to another rule. Rationale: the box knew only each country's number length and a hand-written cleaning rule saved wrong numbers (INC-402); the operator asked whether the box knows each country's format. Decided by the supervisor inside the bundle 3 brief, which the operator approved by pasting it. Status (2026-10-04): built in bundle 3 Part B (`src/features/posting/phone-parse.ts`; PW-125, PW-126, PW-128); on main at 9ba4ff73.

DEC-119 (2026-10-03, operator "We need to make sure we make lovable faster as dicussed and planned earlier.") — SPEED RULES FOR THE EXECUTOR. Issued as "RULINGS 2", replacing the brief's "Local E2E" line and its end-of-bundle local run: each turn starts by reading CI for the executor's last commit, and a red is fixed before new work; local E2E per turn is only the spec files changed or added in that turn, on both projects at 2 workers, with the selector's list added only when it names five spec files or fewer; there is no end-of-bundle local run — a bundle closes on CI green for its final commit; the whole `bun run test:unit`, typecheck, lint and the whole-tree `format:check` before each commit stay; the executor does not stop between parts, only for a migration's apply line, a ruling the brief cannot answer, or the end, and a turn that ends mid-work says three lines: done, left, CI. Decision rule: a CI red in a spec the executor did not run locally that is still red after its next turn sends that area back to the selector's full list for the rest of the bundle. Refined the same night: at most three spec files per turn, and a helper swap across many specs is CI's to prove ("RULINGS 3"); the red-first rule applies to defect fixes only, and a run cancelled by a later push still lists the failures of the shards that finished, which are read as red ("RULINGS 6"). Rationale: a full CI run takes about 22 minutes and already proves the whole suite, while the executor's local runs were being cut off by its turn's time limit. Status (2026-10-04): in force; written into the bundle 4 brief's HOW TO WORK, which cites DEC-119 for the decision rule. No area was recorded as sent back under the rule.

RULINGS. No D-numbered ruling was created in this period. Rulings the supervisor made on the operator's behalf (G17), each reported to him:
- Reserved names: the exemption belongs to the word, not the row; the fold drops separators on both sides and reads look-alike digits as letters, so no joined spellings are added; a handle under five letters counts in rule e only as a whole part between underscores; Amharic keeps the form the page printed; the folded form "coopbank" sits on two rows (Cooperative Bank of Oromia, Co-operative Bank of Kenya) and stays so — at load the first row, the Ethiopian one, keeps it.
- `search_tsv` staying ungranted on `listings` is accepted.
- The step 4 verdicts for the six flagged functions (above).
- Red runs after the fact waived for PW-125 and PW-126, and for PW-130 and PW-131 (new features whose control does not exist on the old screen).
- A later table with a user-id column is declared RESET in the pool map and reaped without asking.
- Not done in bundle 3, by ruling: the comments dropped from `submit_listing` and `set_listing_pin` are restored when the wizard bundle redeclares both functions; the older database-checker warnings wait for the close-out bundle; the brief's step 14 report is folded into the final report, except the step 4 census.
- The share buttons the operator asked for at the last step (Telegram, WhatsApp, Facebook) are recorded for the public-ad-page bundle: the seller taps to share and nothing posts automatically, as he ruled on 2026-10-02.

- Standing from 2026-10-02 and built in this period: no seller may use "ethio" anywhere in a seller name, "even if its ethio_coffee", and the same holds for the business name buyers see, including the Amharic spelling.
- The two countries (DEC-068, 2026-09-16), restated to the operator: the country the seller declares sets defaults such as currency; the country the connection comes from is recorded once by the server at the first post, cannot be edited by the seller, and is what the ad is filed under; a mismatch is a signal for review, never a block.

CATALOG AND CURATOR WORK (cycle C27; every import is made by the operator on the live admin console after the supervisor's audit).
- Batch 18 (curator's change note of 2026-10-03; base 168 categories · 555 definitions · 1,468 links): `c27-b18-categories.csv` (9 rows; `capabilities` = `own_place` on apartments-condos, commercial-property, condominiums, houses, land-plots, new-developments, other-real-estate, roommates-shared, short-term-rentals; expected "changed 9"), `c27-b18-definitions.csv` (5 rows: `part_for`, `brand-beauty`, `product_type-health-wellness`, `brand-solar`, `camera_type`; expected "changed 5"), `c27-b18-links.csv` (the full 1,468 rows; expected "changed 1 · unchanged 1,467"). Content: Part For › Car gains 18 makes (50 → 68 values, the one allowed list above 50; the ceiling is 150); Now Foods joins the beauty Brand list for Supplements & Vitamins; SRNE joins the solar Brand list; Security Camera answers to the finder word "V380", which takes the place of the finder word "Wi-Fi camera" (V380Pro is not on the Brand list); Kirkland stays out. The curator's audit passed. Batch 18 was imported before this period began: the batch 19 note calls the batch-18 state "confirmed live". The import preview lines of batch 18 are not in the sources read for this block.
- Batch 19 ("Negotiable" leaves Pricing Basis): imported by the operator at 15:19 ET on 2026-10-03, links first, each file its own confirm, no Publish needed. `c27-b19-links.csv`: "0 added · 13 changed · 0 unlinked · 0 deleted · 1455 unchanged · 0 refused — Import applied — 13 changes written" (the value `negotiable` taken out of 13 link scopes). `c27-b19-definitions.csv`: "0 added · 1 changed · 0 unlinked · 0 deleted · 0 unchanged · 0 refused — Import applied — 1 changes written" (the option removed from `pricing_type`; eight options remain). The order is reversed on purpose: the scopes must drop the value while it is still an option. The negotiable tick on the price page covers it (DEC-081). Catalogue after batch 19: 168 categories · 555 definitions · 1,468 link rows. Three other lists keep a choice called Negotiable and were left alone (Fuel Included and Insurance Included on vehicle hire; Payment Frequency on the six real-estate leaves).
- Reserved seller names, a research list and not an import file. v1 `c27-reserved-seller-names-2026-10-03.csv`: 435 names and 2,427 handles in six columns (`name_en`, `name_am`, `kind`, `country`, `handles`, `source`) — Ethiopia 223, Kenya 39, Eritrea 11, Djibouti 19, Somalia 40, International 103; kinds bank, insurer, telecom, mobile money, airline, utility, government body, delivery or ride service, money transfer, online platform, email provider; Ethiopia rests on the National Bank's registers read that day (all 32 licensed banks, 18 insurers and the reinsurer, 26 payment issuers and operators, the remittance register of 2026-08-07) and Proclamation 1263/2021 for the 22 ministries. The supervisor's rulings on v1, as the curator holds them: the whole seller name is compared after folding and a second rule refuses a reserved handle standing as a whole part beside only a claim word; handles under five letters serve only the second rule (130 handles on 117 rows); the 35 extra international rows stay; kinds and the filing of Somaliland and Puntland names under Somalia stay; the site's own names are reserved elsewhere. v2 (relayed by the operator at 15:24 ET) adds `exact_only` (yes on 22 rows: the 19 everyday words and three place names — `ride`, `feres`, `seregela`, `fayda`, `imo`, `alibaba`, `ups`, `ems`, `bolt`, `visa`, `stripe`, `threads`, `messenger`, `outlook`, `proton`, `ria`, `wise`, `signal`, `meta`, `awash`, `dashen`, `wegagen`) and `name_am_source` (page 75, rendered 360; all 75 are Ethiopian rows). v3 (relayed at 15:27 ET) puts the exempt word itself in the `exact_only` cell and restores four Amharic names to what the page printed (Goh Betoch Bank, Addis Ababa Water and Sewerage Authority, Ethiopian Food and Drug Authority, Authority for Civil Society Organizations); 435 names, 2,427 handles, eight columns, 2,162 different names after folding. v3 is final (sha256 above), saved as `docs/data/reserved-names-v3.csv` and seeded by M2. The curator's own caveats stand in its notes: 360 of the 435 Amharic names are its own renderings (`name_am_source` = rendered), and "the whole column needs a native proofread before use"; the list was seeded as it is and corrections can be loaded later.
- Price periods in rent and hire ads (curator's research note of 2026-10-03, no rows): per month on the six real-estate leaves with rent or lease; per night on booking sites and per month on Ethiopian classifieds for short-term rentals; per hour for machines, per month for trucks, per day for event gear and generators; per day for vehicle hire; no period printed on most clothing-rental ads; per week and per event are never printed as a price. Three patterns for the form: the price period and the payment schedule are two statements; the minimum term has its own unit; many hire ads state no period. Every category holds the price period "once", locked, today. The note is the base for the rent and hire rows the curator builds after bundle 4.
- Countries. At the end of the period the supervisor drafted a small curator task, "CURATOR: countries file", asking for a countries import file with every ISO 3166-1 country missing from the table, as closed markets, so that any seller could state a home country (INC-413). Outcome on 2026-10-04: the curator read the operator's export `countries_1.csv` and reported that no country is missing — the table already holds all 249 ISO 3166-1 entries, 17 open markets and 232 closed — so no file was needed; it held one file of four currency corrections (Curaçao and Sint Maarten ANG → XCG, Saint Helena GBP → SHP, South Georgia SHP → GBP), which the operator imported on ethio-prod ("4 changed, 0 refused"); none of the twelve unit-system differences it raised was adopted (imperial stays for the United States, Liberia and Myanmar only).
- The curator was otherwise idle after v3, waiting for the engine items INC-374, INC-381 and DEC-094, which are Part G of bundle 4. Pets & Animals stays closed and Jobs and Tenders wait for v2. The curator's batches are loaded through the admin console and are not in the repository; the repository holds the early seed rows only.

THE "WALK FIXES" PROMPT AS DRAFTED (after the 23:19 ET walk; "WALK FIXES (bundle 3, operator walk 2026-10-03). One migration, M4."). CLASS RULES FORGED (instructions/Knowledge amendment proposals — none was written as an amendment or installed in this period; the rules were issued as rulings to the executor and carried into the bundle 4 brief):
- A ledger mark is written only by a migration file, never by hand, on any database; nothing is written to ethio-staging outside the tests' own scratch rows (INC-405).
- A dial is never raised to pass a test; the test gets a `rate_overrides` row for its own user (INC-404).
- A whole redeclaration keeps the body byte for byte apart from the named change, comments included.
- Before every report: the whole `bun run test:unit` and the whole-tree `bun run format:check`, and CI's result on the last commit (INC-406).
- Fix the class once: one helper gives the state every test needs (`leaseSeller`), in place of a call before each use.
- A new table with a user-id column is declared in the pool reset map and reaped, without asking.
- A migration's proofs run in a block that is rolled back and leave nothing behind (the M1b pattern).
- Every new GRANT to anon, authenticated or PUBLIC is named in `scripts/public-surface-allowlist.txt` with a one-line reason (INC-397); a column added to `listings` is private until a migration grants it.
- A test that can no longer be shown red carries a positive control in the same test; red-first applies to defect fixes, not to a new feature whose control does not exist on the old screen.
- A run cancelled by a later push still lists the failures of the shards that finished; they are read as red.
- Because the repository is public, detail on an open security hole enters it only after the fix is live.

S51 · 2026-10-03 → 2026-10-04 · BUNDLE 3 WALK FIXES (M4, M4b, M4c) AND CLOSE OF BUNDLE 3 · COUNTRIES CHECK · BUNDLE 4 "THE POSTING WIZARD" (BRIEF, M5, M5b, M6, TURNS 1–9) · DEC-120–127 · INC-414–431 · RULINGS none numbered · SLIPS none numbered (listed below)

WORDS USED IN THIS BLOCK. A "bundle" is one brief for the executor, saved under `docs/governance/briefs/`, built over several executor turns and closed by a green CI run on its final commit and an operator walk. Bundle 3 is the contact-and-seller-name bundle (security doors, contact step, seller-name rules; its migrations M1, M1b, M2 and M3 are recorded in the previous block). Bundle 4 is the posting-wizard bundle. "M4" to "M6" are the supervisor's running names for the migrations of these two bundles. A "door" is a database function the app calls. A "mark" is the row a migration writes into `public.migration_marks`; the executor ends a migration turn with the line "apply <fragment of the file name> → expect mark <value>" and the operator reads the mark back after applying. A "healer" is a follow-up migration file that only writes a missing ledger row or a later mark, because a landed migration file is never edited. "Red first" means the test is shown failing before the fix lands. A "walk" is the operator's check of the published site against a numbered list written by the supervisor. Commit and CI times are UTC; the operator's messages are dated in his local time (UTC−4).

BUNDLE 4 — THE BRIEF (`docs/governance/briefs/bundle-4.md`, saved unchanged by the executor, sha256 beginning `9ba52ecf`; censuses in `docs/governance/briefs/bundle-4-census.md`). Title: "BUNDLE 4 — THE POSTING WIZARD: STEP ORDER, PRICE PAGE, TITLE, PICTURE, LIFETIME, CONTACT STEP, AND THE CATALOGUE ENGINE (2026-10-04). Tier A. Two migrations: M5 (Parts A to F) and M6 (Part G)." Written on 2026-10-04 at 28 steps, re-based to `b432691b`, then extended to 32 steps with Part F after the re-walk. The operator pasted it into the executor on 2026-10-04 at 02:49 local; by the supervisor's stated rule, "Your paste is the approval", that paste approved the brief with the decisions DEC-120 to DEC-125 and DEC-127 and the overrulable choices listed below. The brief says "The decision and incident ledgers are not written in this bundle", which is why DEC-120 to DEC-127 and INC-414 to INC-431 reach the ledgers only with this record.
- Part A (steps 1–5): the order of the steps and the door numbers (DEC-110, DEC-113, DEC-120); finished steps clickable on wide screens.
- Part B (steps 6–11): the price page owns the deal (DEC-109, DEC-121, DEC-122, DEC-123); one shared searchable picker for the currency and phone-country boxes.
- Part C (steps 12–13): the title is built from the seller's answers (`buildTitle`: card-ranked answers that are answered, visible and not price-page rows, joined by single spaces, stopping before 70 characters; yes/no answers, lists of more than two picks and texts over 30 characters are left out; never an amount, currency, period or price type); the writing helper is never sent the amount or the currency.
- Part D (steps 14–15): one picture component (the ad's first photo, else the nearest parent category's picture, else a neutral icon); the "Photos coming soon" tick and ribbon (`listings.photos_soon`, door `set_listing_photos_soon`).
- Part E (steps 16–20): lifetime (DEC-117; INC-400, INC-414, INC-415); the pin, street line and directions are cleared when the city of the ad's first place changes (INC-393); "This is also my shop or office" (DEC-125); the seller's statement above Publish ("By publishing you confirm that you have the right to sell this, and that the ad follows the rules of ethio.com.", no tick, `listings.attested_at`); three door items (the home-country check moves into the judge at `p_step >= 8`; `get_attribute_options` is gated by a new dial `options_read` 400 per 3600 seconds, INC-410; lost comments restored in `submit_listing` and `set_listing_pin`).
- Part F (steps 21–25): the contact step (INC-422, INC-423 with DEC-127, INC-424, INC-425); step 25 is M5.
- Part G (steps 26–30): the catalogue engine the curator waits on — a row shown only when two answers both match, written `k1=a|b&k2=c|d` in the links file (INC-381); a number range marked `"settled": true` that replaces the question (INC-374); the `{country}` token (DEC-094) and the `{category:<slug>}` pointer (DEC-095), both under DEC-124; step 30 is M6.
