Not done, and parked by the supervisor for the close-out bundle because no written spec for the old codes could be recovered: S1, the "Bundle 1" line (search timing 306 ms at the edge against a 300 ms target, the database at about 11 ms; a ruling is owed by the supervisor), S2/S3, T (with T4), A, B, C, Part O (INC-369, INC-370), the E census (CI still logs 6 "listing not found" lines), the final full DEC-023 run, the D+L+M line (Part O readers not built), the S2 re-time list, DEC-098 stage 2.
- Overrulable choices put to the operator with the brief, none overruled: the home-country check moves into the judge at step 8; lifetime by category only (by plan level later, in the posting-limits bundle); the public card shows the category's picture; the options dial is 400 an hour; the seller's statement has no tick; first and last name are required for a person only, a business needs its business name; published ads keep their contact details when the profile changes; editing the profile outside an ad waits for the Settings bundle.
- Working rules written into the brief: each turn starts by reading CI for the last commit of the turn before it, and a red is fixed first; CI is expected red only between the M5 stop and the turn that lands the screens of Parts A and B; red first only for the defect fixes (steps 16, 17, 22, 23); local E2E per turn is the changed spec files, at most three, both projects, 2 workers, with no end-of-bundle local run (decision rule DEC-119: a CI red in a spec not run, still red after the next turn, sends that area back to the selector's full list); the whole unit run, typecheck, lint and `format:check` before each commit; every function redeclared whole from the live definition; the mark is chosen at apply time (now in UTC on ethio-prod, rounded up to the next hour, plus one hour); `scripts/check-migrations.sh` is run and its "Self-marking guard OK" line pasted before applying; "When a job fails, read those files before anything else; ask the operator for a re-run only when the failed job shows no evidence lines at all."

DEC-120 (2026-10-04, supervisor decision in the bundle 4 brief; approved by the operator's paste of the brief) — THE DOOR'S STEP NUMBERS FOLLOW THE NEW ORDER: 4 IS THE PRICE, 5 IS THE TITLE. A seller walks category → specifications → photos → price → title and description → place → contact → review (the order itself is DEC-110 and DEC-113). The door's numbers follow for the two steps that move: 4 = price, 5 = title and description; steps 1, 2, 3, 6, 7 and 8 keep their meaning. `SEQUENCE` in `src/features/posting/types.ts` becomes `[1, 3, 2, 4, 5, 6, 7, 8]`; test ids keep the form `post-step-<door number>`. `validate_listing_draft` runs the price block at `p_step >= 4` and the title and description block at `p_step >= 5`, defers the price page's rows while `p_step < 4`, and judges a video link's shape at any step so the photos page shows its own refusal. One data fix: a draft stored at `draft_step` 4 (title judged, price not) is set to 3. Rationale: the screen must match the door's step numbers and the door's price-page rows at once, so the screens of Parts A and B land in one turn with every test that names a step by number. Status (2026-10-04): built and landed — door side in M5 (`20261004144146_923dd4cb` → mark `20261004090000`; 8 drafts on ethio-prod moved from step 4 to step 3), screens in bundle 4 turns 1 to 3 (green from CI run 37220875850 on `72cc8102`); the clickable step list and its test PW-135 came in turn 4.

DEC-121 (2026-10-04, supervisor decision in the bundle 4 brief; approved by the operator's paste) — THE PRICE PAGE'S ROWS ARE CHOSEN BY KEY FAMILY, AND THE DOOR DECIDES. New function `deal_group(p_key, p_has_unit)`: `basis` when the key matches `^(pricing_type|unit_of_sale)(-|$)`; `size` when it matches `^(pack_quantity|net_weight_g|volume_ml)(-|$)` and the category links a `unit_of_sale` key; `quantity` when it matches `^quantity_available(-|$)`; `terms` when it matches `^(lease_term|payment_frequency|payment_plan|min_hire_days)(-|$)` or starts with `term_`; otherwise NULL. `deal_keys(p_category_id)` lists a category's price-page keys (server-only). `get_posting_schema` gains a `deal` block with the four lists in display order. The price page draws, top to bottom, "How it is sold" (the basis in force and the visible size rows), "The price", "How many do you have?" and "Terms"; a group with no visible row draws nothing. The specifications page draws every other row. A category with no unit keeps Net Weight, Volume and Pieces per Pack on the specifications page. The line "Sold per <unit> — Change" goes, and N2 (the earlier arrangement in which the unit was asked on the specifications page) is retired: the unit is asked on the price page, for goods as for services. Rationale: "The door decides; the screen reads the door's answer and never keeps its own list." Consequence for the curator: a row on the specifications page must never carry a condition on a price-page row, because the specifications page is answered first. Status (2026-10-04): built and landed — M5 `923dd4cb`; screens in turns 1 to 5 (PW-136 to PW-139; one copy of the specifications form on the price page, one schema read, one fields report); census match: a basis key on 65 of the 151 leaf categories (`unit_of_sale` on 38, `pricing_type` on 27, none with both), `quantity_available` on 29, no `term_` key yet.

DEC-122 (2026-10-04, supervisor decision in the bundle 4 brief; approved by the operator's paste) — A RENT OR HIRE PERIOD IS A CONDITIONAL PRICING BASIS, AND `pricing_type` OUTRANKS `unit_of_sale`. Until now a category could hold one price basis. From now a second one may be linked with a condition (rent and hire: "per month" asked only when the offer is rent). New function `price_basis_in_force(p_category_id, p_attrs, p_prior)` returns `{"key", "ambiguous"}`: the candidates are the category's basis keys whose link condition is met under the answers; one candidate is the key; of several, the one of the `pricing_type` family wins when it is the only one of that family, otherwise the answer is ambiguous (`priceBasisAmbiguous`); none gives null and the period falls back to the category's own. The "required" check applies to the key in force only. The client mirror is one pure function `basisInForce` in `price-basis.ts`. The schema-time `priceBasisAmbiguous` refusal goes: ambiguity is judged with the answers. Rationale: a rent or hire ad needs a period ("per month") that is asked only when the offer is rent, lease or hire, while a sale ad in the same category keeps its unit of sale; the curator's note of 2026-10-03 on price periods for rent and hire (`c27-price-periods-rent-hire-note-2026-10-03.md`) plans those rows. Status (2026-10-04): built and landed (M5 `923dd4cb`, with proofs of the four cases on scratch rows; screens in turns 1 to 5). The curator's rent and hire rows that use it are not written yet: they are in the engine batch after bundle 4 is published.

DEC-123 (2026-10-04, supervisor decision in the bundle 4 brief; approved by the operator's paste) — THE AD STORES ITS UNIT, AND THE UNIT WORDS HAVE A PUBLIC READER. `listings` gains `price_unit` (the option token of the basis in force, for example `per_kg`) and `price_unit_text` (the seller's written unit when the token is `other`), both readable by anon and authenticated; `validate_listing_draft` returns them from the basis in force, `submit_listing` and `edit_listing` store them, M5 backfills existing rows, and a listing with no basis in force holds NULL. New function `price_unit_labels()` (STABLE, SECURITY DEFINER, EXECUTE to anon and authenticated) returns the English and Amharic label of every option of every basis key and nothing else, so the catalogue stays closed (INC-397). One shared price line (`src/features/posting/price-line.ts`) is used by the card, the wizard's preview, the review and the detail: "<amount> <currency> per <unit>" when the listing holds a unit whose label has a noun, else the period, else the amount alone. Under the price the preview, review and detail show the size as one line ("One pack: 12 pieces · 500 g") and each term as "<label>: <value>" (`deal-lines.ts`). Supervisor ruling of 2026-10-04 on the wording: a period price reads "4,100 ETB per day" (Amharic "በቀን") through the same template as a unit, not "4,100 ETB Per day"; a one-off price prints the amount alone and "One-off" is no longer printed. Rationale: the public card must print "per <unit>" although the catalogue is closed to the public (INC-397), so the ad carries its own unit and one reader hands out unit words only. Status (2026-10-04): built and landed (M5 `923dd4cb`; app at dev `f0120a95`; unit tables in `price-line.test.ts` and `deal-lines.test.ts`; PW-137; CI run 37241062194 SUCCESS).

DEC-124 (2026-10-04, supervisor decision in the bundle 4 brief; approved by the operator's paste) — TWO TOKENS IN CATALOGUE TEXT: `{country}` AND `{category:<slug>}`. `{country}` (the token DEC-094 asked for) may stand in a definition's label and help text and in an option's label, in English and Amharic. One renderer, wrapping `catalogText`, replaces it with a country name in the screen's language: the country of the ad's first place when it has one; else the market the seller is browsing (the area cookie); else the seller's confirmed home country; else the words of a new key ("your country"). Stored answers are tokens and do not change; where catalogue labels feed the finder's index the token is removed before indexing. `{category:<slug>}` (the pointer DEC-095 asked for) may stand in a definition's help text; the same renderer draws it as the category's full path in the screen's language, levels joined by " › ", as a button; tapping it asks "Move this ad to <path>? Answers that still fit are kept." and on yes does exactly what choosing that category on step 1 does, then opens the specifications page. The importer and every door that writes help text refuse a token whose slug is not an active category that accepts ads (`unknownCategoryToken:<slug>`); on the screen a token whose category has since gone renders as nothing. The admin screens keep showing a token as it is stored; only the seller's and the buyer's screens draw it. Per-market presets and lists are not part of this decision. Rationale: DEC-094 and DEC-095 had decided that the two tokens exist, and the curator's country-token rows and its 57 pointer lines wait for an engine that draws them; DEC-124 fixes how each token resolves and what the screens do with it. Status (2026-10-04): door side landed in M6 (`20261004212627_e44f20e5` → mark `20261005100000`: the `{category:<slug>}` check sits in `attr_cell_check`, which became STABLE; M6 carries nothing for `{country}`); the screens of both tokens (brief steps 28 and 29) were built in bundle 4 turn 9b, `0fc968e4` (pushed 2026-10-05 00:58:38Z; 33 files, the real change in `wizard.tsx` +137/−13, the rest a re-indent under `CatalogScopeProvider`): one renderer for both tokens in `src/i18n/catalog-tokens.ts` (`catalogPieces` / `drawCatalog` / `catalogWords`, `TOKEN_RE` for `{country}` and `{category:slug}`); `catalog-scope.ts` + `catalog-scope-provider.tsx` (`tokenCountryCode`: the ad's first place → the area cookie → the CONFIRMED home country → null, which draws `post.catalog.yourCountry` "your country" / «አገርዎ»); the wizard resolves the country name (an open market's anchor name through `entityName`, else `Intl.DisplayNames`, else the fallback) and the category path (a postable node, levels joined by " › "); `catalog-words.tsx` draws the pointer as a button (`data-testid="post-category-pointer"`); `category-move-dialog.tsx` asks `post.catalog.moveAsk` "Move this ad to {path}?" / «ይህ ማስታወቂያ ወደ {path} ይዛወር?», `moveKeeps` «አሁንም የሚስማሙ መልሶች ይቀመጣሉ።», `moveYes` «አዛውር»; confirm re-reads `readPostingSchema(target)`, keeps the answers whose key and type the target asks, then `chooseLeaf`; a gone category draws nothing; in labels, options and units a pointer is never drawn. The executor's census found no door needing a change: `attr_cell_check` (M6) checks `{category:<slug>}` in help text only and accepts `{country}` anywhere, `admin_upsert_attribute` and `attr_import_plan` call it (an unknown slug → `badCell` on `help_text_en`, detail `unknownCategoryToken`), `/api/translate` masks every brace token (`/\{[^{}]*\}/g`), and `catalog_find_sweep` and `listings_search_tsv_refresh` do not index catalogue labels. Tests PW-154/155/156 (`{country}` EN, AM, fallback), PW-157/158 (pointer move; gone category), AT-67 (the importer keeps tokens), AT-68 (unknown slug refused), TR-35 (translation keeps `{country}`), unit `catalog-tokens.test.ts` and `catalog-scope.test.ts`; `admin.attributes.link.andNone` "No second condition" / «ሁለተኛ ሁኔታ የለም» (AT-66 asserts it). Verified by diff 2026-10-05 01:15Z, CLEAN. CI on `0fc968e4` (run 37249574367, completed 01:23:48Z) was red on shard 2 only — PW-129 and PW-134 (mobile-360, "the identity read was never held — Expected: 1 Received: 2"): the new `{country}` lookup read the profile before the place's country answered, the executor's own slip, fixed in `24d401f5` (`wizard.tsx` only, +13/−2: the home read waits for `!draft.loading && itemCountryFor === itemPlaceId`); run 37251884569 on `24d401f5` SUCCESS (01:58:19Z), promoted (main `24d401f5`). `roadmap.md`: "[x] Part G step 28 ({country}) screens — PW-154/155/156, AT-67, TR-35" and "[x] Part G step 29 ({category:slug}, help text only) screens — PW-157/158, AT-68".

DEC-125 (2026-10-04, supervisor decision in the bundle 4 brief; approved by the operator's paste) — A SELLER CAN SAVE ONE PLACE AS "MY SHOP OR OFFICE". New table `seller_places` (one row per user: the location, the pin with its precision and zoom, the street line, the directions, the home country code as personal-data tag, `updated_at`; no client access). Three doors for the caller only: `save_seller_place(p_listing_id)` copies the first place, the pin and the two lines from the caller's own listing, `clear_seller_place()`, `my_seller_place()`. The place page shows one tick under the map once a pin or a street line exists: "This is also my shop or office. Use it for my next ads." Ticking saves, unticking clears, and the tick reopens ticked when the saved place equals what the page holds. A new draft carries the saved place first, else the last post's place as before; every rule of the carry stays (a category with its own place never carries; a city change clears the pin). Supervisor ruling of 2026-10-04: a saved place with a street line and no pin carries its street line and directions too. Rationale: not recorded beyond the tick's own words — the saved place is used for the seller's next ads. Status (2026-10-04): built and landed (M5 `923dd4cb`; app at dev `f0120a95` and `57d04428`; PW-143, PW-146; CI run 37241062194 SUCCESS).

DEC-126 (2026-10-04, supervisor ruling; to the operator: "The package update is a real security fix, and declining it was my mistake.") — THE TANSTACK SECURITY UPDATE IS KEPT, AS ITS OWN ITEM. The package change the executor made unannounced in `4446e42b` (`@tanstack/react-router` 1.170.41, `@tanstack/react-start` 1.168.60, `@tanstack/router-plugin` 1.168.42) stays, because it is the patch for CVE-2026-102989 (INC-418). It is treated as its own item with its own checks: the typecheck fix in `src/routes/__root.tsx`, AT-58 (INC-421) and `routeTree.gen.ts`. The earlier ruling to decline the framework update (INC-372) no longer applies to this update. Companion rule sent to the executor and written into the bundle 4 brief: "A package or framework change is never made inside another task: name it first, with the advisory or the reason, and it gets its own turn and its own CI run." It supersedes DEC-092's revert rule (the update reverted on 2026-09-30 under that rule is the same patch) and INC-372's class rule. Rejected: reverting the update a second time. Status (2026-10-04): in force; landed green at `b432691b` (CI run 37181454628). Live on the published site from the first Publish after that commit; the record shows no Publish on 2026-10-04, and the first recorded Publish after `b432691b` is the operator's Publish of turn 10 (`a17b227c`) on 2026-10-05 at ~03:35Z, followed by the Publish of turn 11 (`70e16ea5`) at ~05:45Z.

DEC-127 (2026-10-04, operator at the re-walk: "also make sure sellers name generation a well as first and last name requirment?"; supervisor decision offered as overrulable and approved by the operator's paste of the brief) — A SELLER IS NAMED BEFORE AN AD IS PUBLISHED. Every seller must hold a public (seller) name; a person must also hold a first name and a last name; a business must hold a business name and may leave the two personal names empty. The rule sits in the judge `validate_listing_draft` at `p_step >= 8`, beside the home-country check, with fields `alias`, `first_name`, `last_name`, `business_name` and reason `required`; publishing runs the judge, and so does editing a live ad (the executor's choice, accepted: an unnamed seller, or one whose home country is not confirmed, is refused on editing a live ad with the same fields as on publish). Saves on the contact step itself (`p_step` 7) are not refused; the page's own Next holds the seller at the empty box. Live ads are not touched; the rule applies at the next publish. The operator may still require the personal names for businesses too; he has not asked for it. Rationale: until this decision nothing required a name, although the documentation said the door did (INC-423). Status (2026-10-04): built and landed (M5 `923dd4cb`; screens at dev `aeac0bfa`; PR-24, PW-148; CI run 37241062194 SUCCESS).

BUNDLE 4 — MIGRATIONS:
- M5 (`20261004144146_923dd4cb-91bb-43a1-9c0d-e4b418c23c23.sql`, 2,170 lines, 110 ASSERTs; declared mark `20261004090000`) is the door side of Parts A to F in one file. Redeclared whole from live: `validate_listing_draft`, `submit_listing`, `edit_listing`, `publish_listing` (stamps `attested_at`; its own home-country block removed because the judge now holds it), `get_posting_schema`, `get_attribute_options` (VOLATILE on purpose: it writes the `options_read` meter), `transition_listing`, `renew_listing`, `expire_stale_listings`, `set_listing_pin` (comments restored, body unchanged), `alias_rule`, `business_name_rule`. Data fixes: `draft_step` 4 → 3, `expires_at`, the `price_unit` backfill. The name-rule timings before and after are measured inside the file and stored in `audit_log` under action `migration.m5_timing`, because the executor's read-only role cannot run `alias_rule` (INC-425 holds the numbers).
- How M5 was applied. Three red-first tests were committed first (`61b02390`: PR-23 for step 16, PR-24 for step 22, PW-133 for step 23 — 6 failed of 6 on both screen sizes against the old door). The executor sent an empty placeholder file to ethio-prod in error (`20261004072852_d59800cd`, one comment line; M5 writes its ledger row `20261004072852`, as M2 did for `60b466e2`). The executor's platform then showed the operator an approval dialog for the database change; it waited about seven hours, until the operator asked on 2026-10-04 at 10:40 local whether to allow or skip, and the supervisor ruled "Allow it." (the file reaches the repository only after the approval, so the review came afterwards). Because of the wait the file's name stamp `20261004144146` is later than its declared mark, which the self-marking guard refuses (the commit that carries M5, `7317616d`, is titled "Applied M5 with guard failure"; its CI run 37210348504 failed).
- M5b (`20261004144628_02084273`, mark `20261004160000`; commit `2ff58122`) is the healer for that: it writes a mark later than its own stamp and changes nothing else; `scripts/migration-mark-allowlist.txt` names M5 and this healer.
- M5 and M5b are on ethio-prod (through the executor's migration tool, after the operator's approval) and on ethio-staging (applied by the operator, who did so before the supervisor's after-the-fact review although he had been asked to wait; no defect was found afterwards). The review at dev `e5127367` compared every redeclared function with its previous declaration and found only the intended changes. Slips noted: the stale mark; M5's proof block again borrowed the oldest real account (rolled back) despite the close-out ruling; the step 23 screen landed before the screens of Parts A and B. CI run 37210551317 on `2ff58122` is recorded CANCELLED; the wizard specs were red in the shards that finished (38 failures), which was the expected window of the old screens against the new door.
- M6 (`20261004212627_e44f20e5-6ff8-46c6-bd2f-75bb6075dee1.sql`, 2,367 lines; declared mark `20261005100000`, chosen at least twelve hours ahead so that a delayed approval still passes the guard) is the door side of Part G (brief steps 26, 27 and 29). It redeclares nine functions whole from live — `attr_visible_when_ok`, `attr_visible_when_met`, `attr_cell_check` (IMMUTABLE → STABLE, because it now reads categories), `attr_option_shape`, `attr_link_cells_refusal`, `attr_export_payload`, `attr_import_plan`, `validate_listing_attributes`, `get_posting_schema` (drops `price_basis_key`, which no code read) — with no DROP, no table change and no stored-row change. It carries: one optional `and` pair on a row condition (shape `{"key": k1, "in": [...], "and": {"key": k2, "in": [...]}}`, one level only, `k2` different from `k1`); `"settled": true` on an option's bounds, accepted only as the boolean true with both `min` and `max` (otherwise `boundsSettledNeedsRange`), under which a required number is not required when absent and a sent value is still judged against the range; the refusal `unknownCategoryToken` for a `{category:<slug>}` token whose category is missing, switched off or not taking ads. Its proofs run on scratch rows in a rolled-back block and use no real account. The supervisor's review accepted it; noted and not blocking: `attr_visible_when_met` now returns true for a non-object condition (such a value cannot be stored, a CHECK refuses it), and the file does not itself prove `attr_import_plan`'s two-pair parse or `attr_export_payload`'s `&` output (test AT-65 does). The executor added seven helper functions to `scripts/public-surface-allowlist.txt`, a file outside the named scope, and said so; accepted, because each already had EXECUTE for `authenticated` in migrations of 2026-09-19 to 2026-10-03, and recorded as a close-out census item (should these stay client-callable).
- How M6 was applied. The executor cannot apply an existing migration file ("The migration tool would write a second copy of the M6 file under a new stamp"), so the operator applied it himself in the Supabase SQL editor: on ethio-staging on 2026-10-04 between 17:37 and 18:08 local, and on ethio-prod at about 19:01 local (the executor later read the mark as written at 23:01 UTC), where his read-back of `select max(version) from public.migration_marks;`, reported at 19:05 local, was `20261005100000`. The executor's commit `83da74b2` is titled "Applied M6 to ethio-prod", which is wrong: that commit changed only the two translation-map files.

S52 · 2026-10-04 → 2026-10-05 · LEGAL: THE 18+ RULE, SPEC L1 (LEGAL DOCUMENTS, ACCEPTANCE, PUBLISHING CERTIFICATION), SPEC L2 (RETENTION), TERMS AND PRIVACY DRAFTS IN ENGLISH AND AMHARIC, REVIEW OF THE 2020 DOCUMENTS · CURATOR C28 (CATERING RESEARCH) AND C29 (CATERING ROWS) · RECORDS DIRECTIVE · DEC-128–130 · RULINGS none numbered · SLIPS none numbered (listed below)

WHERE THE LEGAL TEXTS ARE. On 2026-10-04, while the executor worked on bundle 4, the operator and the supervisor settled the legal basis of the site. The supervisor wrote the texts in a working document titled "ethio.com legal drafts v1 (pre-counsel)" with six tabs: Read me first (the publishing statement in English and Amharic, the table of settled points, the lawyer's list, the list of what is not built); Terms of Service (17 sections); Terms of Service (Amharic); Privacy Policy (15 sections); Privacy Policy (Amharic); Review of the 2020 documents. The texts are exported separately with this records package (the package places them under `docs/spec/legal/`). This block records the decisions, the operator's rulings and the open items, not the texts. All of it is pre-counsel: no lawyer has reviewed any of it, and the supervisor said so each time ("I'm not a lawyer"). The English text is the binding one. When the Legal section of the admin console is built (DEC-129), each text becomes version 1 of its document there.

THE DAY IN ORDER (operator's local time, 2026-10-04). 16:40 the operator asks whether the system restricts age. 16:50 "agree with your recommendations. please proceed as suggested" → DEC-128. 17:01 he agrees to the first spec for Terms, Privacy and the 18+ acceptance and adds admin-managed versions, recorded acceptance and a certification before Publish → spec L1, second version. 17:11 "approve" → DEC-129; he asks how long a posting must be kept. 17:25 he rules on retention → DEC-130; the English drafts are delivered with eleven open points. 17:51 and 18:02 he answers the open points. 18:15 he says he has reviewed the pre-counsel drafts and agrees, and asks for a review of the two pages on the live site; 18:17 he attaches them. 18:36 he answers the review's three questions and asks for the right to act without notice and a very strong disclaimer. 18:44 "OK, AGREE WITH IT. PROCEED." on the rewritten Terms 5 and 12; the Amharic text is then written.

WHAT THE APP HAS TODAY (census on dev, 2026-10-04): no age rule, no date-of-birth column or field, no `/terms` or `/privacy` routes (the footer shows the two words with no pages behind them, `app-footer.tsx:23`), no acceptance at sign-up, and nothing about terms, privacy or age on `docs/governance/launch-gate.md`. No general email send path exists (only the sign-up and password emails of Supabase Auth); the notifications pipeline (REQ-031) is not built. The admin console has no legal or pages section (its sections are users, roles, audit, places, countries, coverage, categories, attributes, images, translations). On the server the listing statuses `sold`, `expired` and `removed` exist, with `relist_listing`; `delete_draft` is the only hard delete; nothing is ever purged; there is no "My ads" screen. The door `admin_set_account_status` exists (account status `active`, `frozen`, `banned`). No app table holds an IP address or a user agent; the category finder hashes the caller's address (sha256) for its rate limit; the security check at sign-up (Turnstile) is a seam only (`auth-service.ts:77`); there are no analytics or trackers in `src`; photo EXIF data is stripped (`src/server/media/strip.ts`); the cookies are `ethio_lang_star` and `ethio_area`; the map-pin tool reads the device's position when the seller taps "Use my location" (`map-pin-dropper.tsx:297–302`).

DEC-128 (2026-10-04, operator "agree with your recommendations. please proceed as suggested") — 18 OR OLDER, CONFIRMED WITH THE ACCEPTANCE; NO DATE OF BIRTH IS COLLECTED. A user must be 18 or older to hold an account, post or message. That rule was already in the record (decided 2026-07-29: Q-018 and clause A13 of the source ledger `docs/spec/tos-privacy-source.md:31`, "accounts found under 18 are closed"); DEC-128 settles how it is applied. No date of birth is collected anywhere. The 18+ confirmation is part of the single acceptance tick, "I am 18 or older and I agree to the Terms and the Privacy Policy.", stored with its time; existing accounts confirm once at their next sign-in. The admin's deactivate action gains a preset reason "Under 18", audited as today. The report feature (REQ-026), when built, carries a matching reason ("appears to be under 18"). The notes on each country's law go to the source ledger and to the existing Counsel item of the launch checklist. The standing counsel ruling is kept: a lawyer reviews at the Ethiopia-entity milestone and that review does not block global launch; the supervisor's own suggestion of "a lawyer's read before launch" conflicted with that ruling and was withdrawn, and the operator, told of it, did not change the ruling. Launch-checklist additions: a children's access assessment for the United Kingdom within three months of opening the UK, which the supervisor drafts; a re-check of the United Arab Emirates child digital safety law before its grace period ends. Rationale, as given to the operator: a date of birth "is personal data you would have to protect, no peer asks for it at sign-up, and once you collect it you know which users are minors and must act on each one. Ask for it only if paid features bring identity checks later." Peers, from the terms pages of eleven marketplaces read on 2026-10-04: eight set 18 (eBay, Facebook Marketplace, Gumtree, Etsy, Vinted, OLX, Jiji, Dubizzle), OfferUp sets 16, Craigslist and Kijiji state no number; none shows a date of birth at basic sign-up (it appears only for payment onboarding or optional and triggered checks); only Vinted describes a document-based age check. Law, as the supervisor read it that day: a person under 18 generally cannot be bound by terms (in Ethiopia a minor is under 18); the United States children's privacy law applies only to a site aimed at children or one that actually knows a user is under 13; the United Kingdom's Online Safety Act covers a listings site with messaging and requires the access assessment; the EU's platform rules do not oblige a site to collect data to find minors and exempt small companies from those sections; Ethiopia's Personal Data Protection Proclamation 1321/2024 treats under-16s as minors, needs a parent's consent for their data and asks for "reasonable efforts to verify the age"; Kenya's Data Protection Act (section 33) asks for "appropriate mechanisms for age verification"; South Africa's POPIA needs a parent's or guardian's consent under 18; the UAE law came into force in January 2026 with a one-year grace period, names online marketplaces and asks for age verification by risk class (the supervisor could not confirm that the risk classes have been issued); Australia's under-16 rule is not aimed at marketplaces. "No law found in any of your open markets requires collecting a date of birth as such." Rejected: asking for a date of birth at sign-up; making a lawyer's read a new launch blocker. Parked for counsel: whether a self-declaration meets Ethiopia's and Kenya's wording. Status (2026-10-04): decided, not built — the acceptance is built with spec L1 (DEC-129), right after bundle 4; the record lines ride Part H of bundle 4 (turn 10).

DEC-129 (2026-10-04, operator "approve. also make sure that we record in each user profile for the version agreeing terms of service.") — LEGAL DOCUMENTS ARE MANAGED AND VERSIONED IN THE ADMIN CONSOLE, EVERY ACCEPTANCE IS RECORDED, AND EVERY PUBLISH, EDIT AND RENEW IS CERTIFIED (SPEC L1). The operator's request that shaped it, in his words: "we should have admin side where we from time to time modify these terms of service. so nees to have versions and each user when signing in signs and that is docuemnted when they signed and what version visible only to admin side (if not legally required) what version they signed and when an what time recorded. when Terms of service is updated we will send email then next signin need to agree with that as well. now at the end of posting before clicking publish, will there be a certification saying they certify that all the information is true and they will not hold ethio.com responsible or Liable and that they are the whole responsible person or company as well. more over each countries law may be different and we may have different certifications based on each country that we wants user to certify before bublishing, as well as updating a post, etc and that recorded with each post as well?" What was decided:
- A "Legal" section in the admin console. Three kinds of document: Terms, Privacy Policy, Publishing statement. Each has numbered versions with its text in English and Amharic, a change summary, an effective date, and who published it and when. A published version can never be edited or deleted; a change is a new version. Publishing needs its own permission, a fresh sign-in check and both languages, and is audited. A document is global or belongs to one country. `/terms` and `/privacy` show the version in force with its date; the footer's two words become links. The legal text therefore lives in the database, not in the repository.
- Acceptance at sign-in. Any signed-in account that has not accepted the version in force gets one screen with one required tick: "I am 18 or older and I agree to the Terms and the Privacy Policy." It covers the three sign-in doors (email, Google, Telegram), existing accounts, and re-acceptance when a document changes. Browsing stays open without it; posting, saving a seller profile and, later, messaging need it. The sign-up page carries one line with the two links and no tick: the acceptance screen is the single recorded act. Recorded per acceptance: the user, the document, the version, the time (UTC), the language shown and the sign-in method. No IP address is stored (the supervisor's call, to keep personal data minimal; the operator was invited to say if he wants it stored as evidence and has not answered). The record is append-only and written only through its own door for the signed-in user; staff acting for a user cannot accept for them; the record carries the home country, for the Ethiopia partition. It is shown on the admin's user page only — the user has no history screen — but it is included in the copy of their data a user may formally ask for (a right in the EU, the UK, Ethiopia, Kenya and South Africa) and in deletion. By the operator's addition, the profile row also carries the latest accepted Terms version and Privacy version with their times, and the full history sits in a table beside it.
- When a document changes. The admin marks a version as a material change or not; a typo fix asks nobody for anything. A material change takes effect on a date the admin sets; the default notice is 30 days (the spec first said 14; the supervisor raised it on 2026-10-04 because the Terms on the live site since 2020 promise 30; it is a setting and the operator was told he can say 14). From that date the acceptance screen returns at the next sign-in. A user who does not accept can still browse and cannot post, edit or renew ads or contact sellers. The email the operator asked for goes out when the version is published; that part depends on the notifications pipeline and on the sending domain, so until it is built the acceptance screen is the notice, and the launch checklist gets the line that the email must be live before the first material change after launch.
- Publishing certification. Above Publish the seller sees the statements that apply to this ad: the global one, plus one for each country where the ad is shown and for the seller's home country, where such a statement exists; then one required tick, "I certify the above." (Amharic «ከላይ ያለውን አረጋግጣለሁ።»). It is required on publish, on edit and on renew. The server decides which statements apply and refuses if the screen confirmed a different set. Recorded per ad and per action: the ad, the user, the action (publish, edit or renew), the statement versions, the countries, the time and the language; the admin sees it on the ad's page; staff helping a user can prepare an ad and cannot certify for them; the record is kept and erased with its ad. It replaces the line without a tick that bundle 4 step 19 put above Publish and the `listings.attested_at` stamp. The global statement has five points (the text is in the legal export): everything in the ad is true and complete and the photos show what is offered; the seller has the right to offer the item or service and to use the photos and text, and offering it breaks no law where the ad is shown; the ad follows the Terms and the posting rules; "I alone, or the business I act for, am responsible for this ad and for any deal that follows from it."; "ethio.com only displays the ad. It is not a party to any deal and is not liable for it, as the Terms of Service set out." Caution recorded with it: how far the fifth point protects ethio.com differs by country, EU and UK consumer law limit it, and a country statement can narrow or extend it for one country.
- No fact is typed into the legal text (operator: every named fact "SHOULD ALSO BE UPDATABLE DYNAMICALLY- A MUST FOR ALL"). The operator's legal name and address, the contact emails, the list of providers, the liability amount, the notice days, the response time and the retention periods are legal settings in the admin console. Each published version freezes the values it went out with, and changing a setting creates a new version, so text that people already accepted never changes silently. The retention figures come from the same settings the erasure job uses, so the policy cannot drift from what the system does.
- Translation (operator: "WE SHOULD BE ABLE TO DYNAMICALLY TRANSLATE ALL IN DIFFERENT LANGUAGES AS WELL"). Each version is machine-translated into every active site language; a translator can correct a translation without creating a new version; English is the binding text; the page says when a translation is automatic. The supervisor still wants the operator, or a native speaker, to read the Amharic, since it is a primary language.
- Two additions by the supervisor, reported to the operator on 2026-10-04 and not objected to, "because strong words only help if the user saw them": the acceptance screen shows three short lines above the tick (we do not verify ads; you deal at your own risk; we may remove ads or close accounts without notice); and every ad carries a fixed line beside the seller's contact, "ethio.com does not verify sellers or ads. Inspect before you pay." (the buyer banner of REQ-028; no such line exists in `en.ts` today).
- Server rules and tests: nobody can write another user's acceptance or certification; a published version cannot be changed; an ad cannot be published, edited or renewed without the current certification; publishing and the seller-profile save are refused without a current acceptance; a new account at each door reaches the screen, accepts and posts; a new version brings the screen back; test accounts get an acceptance by default, which changes the test setup and so "carries its own decision record".
Points of the first version of the spec that the second did not restate — the footer links, the line on the sign-up page, browsing staying open without acceptance, the append-only record written through its own door, and the forward-scan against later requirements (the per-post affirmation, cross-border consent kept separate, staff unable to accept for a user, the home country on the record) — are recorded above as carried over; the second version says only that its tests and server rules are "as in the first version, plus" three more. Tier A. ESTIMATE (labelled): four to six executor turns and two migrations (the first version was estimated at two to three turns). Rationale: the operator wants to change the terms from time to time and to be able to show who accepted which version and when, and who certified each ad; the first version of the spec (text in the repository with a version string, one line above Publish) could not do either. Rejected: the first version of the spec; storing the IP address with the acceptance; an arbitration clause in the Terms (eBay has one; left for the lawyer). Status (2026-10-04): decided, not built — queued as the first task after bundle 4 ("the legal section, L1"). Until it is built the step 19 line and `attested_at` stand, and none of the legal texts is published.

DEC-130 (2026-10-04, operator "12 or 6 months after deletion - best to make uniform for all 12 months instead of deviding per jerusdiction to make it simple for Deleted by the seller. other recomendation agree") — HOW LONG ADS, ACCOUNTS, MESSAGES AND TECHNICAL RECORDS ARE KEPT (SPEC L2). The periods, the same in every country:
- An ad marked sold or no longer available: its page stays 30 days with a ribbon, the contact hidden, out of feeds and search; the seller sees it in My ads and can relist; after the 30 days it leaves public view and stays in My ads for 12 months.
- An expired ad: gone from public view at once; in My ads for 12 months, where it can be relisted; then archived as a deleted ad.
- An ad deleted by the seller: gone at once for the public and for the seller; a copy is kept out of sight for 12 months, uniformly; then erased.
- An ad that was reported, enforced against, or is under an official request: kept until the case closes, plus 3 years; then erased.
- A closed account: all its ads go; the same periods apply; the account's identity is kept 12 months; a banned account keeps a minimal record.
- Messages follow the ad they are about (operator, 18:02: "12 MIONTHS I THINK, 3 YEARS IF ANY LEGAL DISPUTE- PLEASE LET ME KNOW, I THINK IT SHOULD FOLLOW WHAT WE SAID ABOUT ALL WHAT WE SAID ABOUT POSTING"): 12 months after the ad is deleted, and until a report, dispute or official request closes, plus 3 years.
- Technical records (network address, browser and device type and the time, per sign-in session): 12 months — the supervisor's call of 2026-10-04, reported to the operator as a setting he can change. The erasure job must also cover the Supabase auth session and audit rows that hold addresses. The hosting companies' own server logs follow those companies' periods.
- Mechanics: the periods are admin settings; a hold flag stops erasure for any ad or account under a report, a dispute or an official request; a scheduled job erases when a period ends and records each run (a heartbeat); the archive is restricted — only staff with a specific permission can open it and every view is audited; the publishing certification is kept and erased with its ad; the Privacy Policy states the periods.
The operator's request, in his words (17:11): "when posting also need to see how long we need to keep posting legally even if user says sold(marking when a post is sold, or no longer available, may be 30 days after that will remain as sold or no longer available ribbon, then if the user deletes an advert, it may delete from the users list but legally we may have to possibly keep it for some time i suspect for a year or so or may e different by jurisdiction. so we need to reaserch and has to come up a way". Rationale: no country the supervisor checked requires keeping an ad after it is sold or deleted, so the period is the operator's choice inside the privacy laws' limit of "no longer than necessary". The fixed duties found cover logs and identity, not ad content: Ethiopia's Computer Crime Proclamation 958/2016 (article 24) has service providers keep traffic data for one year (whether a classifieds site is a "service provider" is unclear); France (decree 2021-1362) has hosts keep the identity of who posted, with account data for one year after closure; Lebanon, traffic data three years (one secondary source). Authorities can order data preserved: Ethiopia 3 + 3 months, Kenya 30 days, South Africa 21 to 90 days, the United States 90 + 90 days, the EU 60 + 30 days. Reported child-abuse material is kept one year in the United States and the United Kingdom. Civil claims can be brought for 3 years (Germany, South Africa) up to 15 (UAE), and regulators do not accept keeping everything that long. Peers: most remove a sold ad from search at once (eBay keeps sold items findable for about 90 days; Kijiji shows "no longer available"); where a period after deletion is stated it is short — 50 days (Kleinanzeigen), 90 days (OLX Pakistan), 6 months (Vinted; Gumtree for images) — and Kleinanzeigen keeps data three years where fraud is suspected. Rejected: the supervisor's proposal of 6 months for sellers whose home country is in the EU, the UK, Norway or Switzerland, and with it any split by country. Parked for the Ethiopia compliance matrix (REQ-035) and the counsel list, not built now: keeping sign-in and posting event logs 12 months for users in Ethiopia, the supervisor's cautious reading of the one-year rule. Status (2026-10-04): decided, not built — built with the My ads screens (mark sold, relist, renew, delete), after L1. It finalises clause B9 of the source ledger ("Pass 2 to finalize"), the "purge per retention rules (Section 5)" of REQ-022, and question Q-012.

OPERATOR RULINGS ON THE LEGAL TEXT (2026-10-04, in his words; each is applied in the drafts):
- Who operates the site: "the name is ethio.com llc and its organized in GEORGIA, USA"; "REGISTERED ADDRESS: 300 COLONIAL CENTER PARKWAY, STE 100N, ROSWELL GA 30076 USA." The drafts read "Ethio.com LLC, a limited liability company organized in the State of Georgia, United States", with that address; the two pages on the live site use the same name and address. Still asked of him: check the spelling of the name against the registration. He also said "some of these legal names need to be dynamic that we can change" (DEC-129, legal settings).
- Governing law. He asked: "ADVISE ME IF ITS BEST TO DO IT IN OTHER STATES? WHEN POSTING IN ETHIOOPIA DO WE NEED TO ORGANIZE IN ETHIOPIA? HOW DO OTHERS DO IT." Advice given and taken into the drafts: keep Georgia (a company formed in Delaware or Wyoming and run from Georgia must still register in Georgia; Delaware mainly matters when raising venture capital); the Terms say Georgia law without its conflict-of-law rules, the state and federal courts located in Georgia, and that a user writes to legal@ethio.com first and allows 30 days before a claim; as far as the supervisor knows no Ethiopian company is needed for people in Ethiopia to post on a free site run from the United States (Jiji Ethiopia's terms name "Jiji Online Limited" under the laws of Gibraltar), while doing business on the ground — taking payments in Ethiopia, staff or agents, an office, or registering with the data regulator to store data in the country — would need one, which the plan already places at the Ethiopia-entity step with a lawyer's review; one risk named: Ethiopia's data law says data collected there should be stored there and can reach foreign operators, and the operator's earlier ruling accepted launching on one database and adding the Ethiopia database later.
- Where data is stored: "YES AT LAUNCH WE MOST LIKLEY WILL NOT HAVE COUNTRY SPECIFIC LAUNCH BUT WE MAY DO IT IN A YEAR OR 2 DEPENDING ON TRAFFIC ETC." The Privacy Policy says personal data is stored on servers in the United States and "We may later store the data of users in some countries, Ethiopia first, on servers inside those countries. This policy will be updated before that happens." The July design note "Ethiopian users' data is stored in Ethiopia" is the future design; a policy must say what is true on the day it is published, and a new version is published when the Ethiopia database goes live.
- No sale of data, no sharing with advertisers, no marketing email: "YES COINFIRM".
- The three clauses the supervisor added that the source ledger does not hold (permission to display users' content; protection of the ethio.com name and design; the seller covering claims caused by their ad): "YES, NEED TO ADD."
- Posting ads on social media: "ALSO BECAUSE WE WILL AUTOMATICALLY POST THESE IN SOCIAL MEDIAS LIKE TELEGARM, FACEBOOK WHATSUP ETC. NEED TO HAVE THAT PERMISSION AGREEN, NO OPT OUT. i DONT KNOW OPT OUT IS REQUIRED OR NEEDED." Applied: the Terms give ethio.com permission to show ads on its own channels and pages on other services (Telegram, Facebook, WhatsApp, Instagram, TikTok) and in search engines, as a condition of posting with no switch per ad; copies are removed when an ad ends where the other service allows. The supervisor read this as ethio.com's own channels, not the sellers' accounts, and said so: "Your earlier ruling that sellers share to their own accounts themselves still stands. Correct me if you meant otherwise." He has not corrected it. An opt-out is not required for the permission; in the EU and the UK a person can still object or delete their ad. The feature itself has no spec.
- Mailboxes legal@ethio.com and privacy@ethio.com: "YES, WILL CREAT" (the drafts also name security@ethio.com).
- Named providers, Resend for email and Lovable for hosting: "FOR NOW BUT SHOULD ALSO BE UPDATABLE DYNAMICALLY- A MUST FOR ALL". The Privacy Policy names Supabase, Cloudflare, Google (sign-in, and Gemini for screening and drafting), Telegram, OpenStreetMap, Resend and Lovable.
- Liability limit of 100 US dollars: "OK".
- Time to answer a data request: "MAKE IT 60 IF LAWS ALLOW US, TO GIVE US FLAEXIBILITY." Applied as 30 days, with up to 60 days more for a complex request, and sooner where a country's law requires it: a flat 60 days is not allowed in the EU or the UK (one month, extendable by two), and Kenya's limits are 7 days for a copy and 14 for a correction or deletion.
- Two things he wants built: "MAY NEED TO BUILD A WAY TO REPORT EACH POSTING OR A WAY TO REQUEST DATA AND VERIFY ETC." Recorded as a Report button on every ad (reasons to include "appears to be under 18" and copyright; it replaces the address in Terms 15; the report feature is REQ-026) and a "Your data" page (request a copy or a deletion, identity confirmed by a fresh sign-in; a person who cannot sign in writes to privacy@ethio.com and is checked by hand; REQ-012.4). Until each is built, reports go to legal@ethio.com and data requests are handled by hand through privacy@ethio.com.
- The drafts as a whole (18:15): "i REVIEWED THE PRECOUNSEL v 1 DOCUMENT AND i AGREE".
- Gambling, betting and lotteries: "BAN THEM". The line stays in Terms 4; the banned-everywhere list of REQ-028 gains it; a screening rule follows when moderation is built.
- The copyright agent's phone number: "I DONT WANT TO POST A PHONE NUMBER BUT IF THERE IS A BETTER WAY, PLEASE LET ME KNOW". Applied: Terms 15 shows the agent's address and legal@ethio.com and no phone number, as on the terms pages of Craigslist, eBay and OfferUp.
- Registering the copyright agent with the United States Copyright Office: "NOT YEST, YOU WILL GUIDE ME HOW I SHOULD DO IT." It costs 6 US dollars and is renewed every three years; the form asks for a phone number, for which a separate number that only takes voicemail is to be used; the agent may be a job title or department or a third-party service. Without the registration the protection United States law gives a platform against copyright claims over what its users post does not apply.
- The right to act without notice: "MAY BE BEST TO DECLARE WE CAN TERMINATE WITHOUT NOTICE, WE CAN REMOVE ADVERTS, RESTRICT OR DELETE ACCOUNTS WITHOUT NOTICE MAY NEED TO BE ADDED EVEN THOUGH WE HAVE PROCESSES AND STEPS. BUT FOR LEGAL PROTECTION AND IF LAWS ALLOWS IT, ITS PROBABLY BEST." Applied in Terms 5 ("Our right to act": ethio.com may remove or hide any ad, and restrict, suspend or close any account, at any time, with or without notice, for any reason or for no stated reason, at its sole discretion; it has no duty to screen and is not liable for screening or not screening; the steps, reasons and appeal are its practice, not a promise; where a country's law requires a reason or notice, it gives it). The supervisor's note with it: in the United States the wording holds; in the EU and the UK a platform must tell a user why it acted and a no-notice term against a consumer can be struck as unfair, so the steps he approved stay, as practice.
- A strong disclaimer: "WE SHOULD DECLARE THAT WE ARE NOT RESPONSIBLE FOR THE CONTENT OF ADVERTIZED AND WE WONT VERIFY OR ETC, AND ITS USERS RESPONSIBILITY TO VERIFY AND WE ARE NOT LIABLE FOR THAT ETC- NEED TO BE VERY STRONG AND NEED TO REVIEW WHAT OTHER MARKELPLACE LIKE CRIAGELIST OR OTHERS SAY OR DO". Applied in Terms 12, renamed "Your responsibility and our liability": every ad comes from the user who posted it and is not verified, endorsed or guaranteed; ethio.com does not verify identity, ownership, quality, safety or legality, and passing its screening proves none of these; users deal at their own risk and check for themselves before paying; ethio.com is not a party to any deal and not liable for loss, injury or dispute; users release ethio.com from disputes between users; implied warranties and indirect losses are excluded, beside the 100 dollar limit. The supervisor's note with it: no wording covers everything — it does not protect a site that is told about an illegal ad and leaves it up, nor against copyright claims until the agent is registered.
- On the rewritten Terms 5 and 12 (18:44): "OK, AGREE WITH IT. PROCEED."

THE REVIEW OF THE 2020 DOCUMENTS. The live site's pages `https://ethio.com/terms-of-use/` and `https://ethio.com/privacy-policy/`, both last updated 14 December 2020, are a shop template (orders, cards, subscriptions, in-app purchases, advertising trackers). The supervisor's reader could not open them (it timed out fetching the site's robots file five times that day, and the supervisor did not route around it), so the operator attached both. Result, part by part: of the old Terms' 34 parts, 15 are adopted in whole or in part, 9 were already covered, 1 is replaced and 9 are dropped; of the old Privacy Policy's 26 parts, 8 are adopted, 5 were already covered, 4 are replaced and 9 are dropped. Carried over: the full copyright notice procedure, with the counter-notice the old text lacked and a named agent; the exclusion of indirect losses and implied warranties; bans on hate, threats, other people's personal information and spam; the seller's confirmation that the photos and text are theirs to use; general clauses (write to us first, invalid parts, no waiver, English prevails, a United States sanctions line); in the Privacy Policy, the disclosure of address and browser records and lines on a sale of the business, Do Not Track and how to delete your data. Dropped: everything about orders, payments and subscriptions, and the old policy's tracking and selling of data ("we may sell and may have sold" personal data; Google, Twitter and Facebook remarketing), which is the opposite of what the operator confirmed. The old "for any reason, without notice" termination was first replaced by steps, reasons and an appeal, and then, on the operator's ruling above, adopted with limits. The two 2020 pages come down on the day the new site replaces the old one; if old accounts are carried over, each is asked to accept version 1 at its first sign-in.

WHAT OTHER MARKETPLACES SAY (terms pages read 2026-10-04; in the review tab). Craigslist: moderates at its sole discretion with no notice promised and is "not liable for moderating or not moderating"; California law, courts in San Francisco; liability 100 dollars or the fees paid in the past year. eBay: may end the service to anyone for any reason at its discretion; no control over and no guarantee of items, listings or sellers; a release for disputes between users; Utah law and arbitration; 100 dollars. OfferUp: may remove content at any time, for any reason, with or without notice; not a party to any deal; a release; the greater of 100 dollars or 12 months of fees. Jiji Ethiopia: with or without notice, ads deleted without notice or explanation; no control and no guarantee; the lesser of six months of fees or ETB 3,400; operated by "Jiji Online Limited" under the laws of Gibraltar. All four keep the right to remove ads and close accounts without notice and all four disclaim the ads; none of Craigslist, eBay and OfferUp prints a phone number for its copyright agent.

WHAT THE DRAFTS DESCRIBE THAT IS NOT BUILT ("Each item below must be live, or its clause removed, before the text is published."): versioned acceptance at sign-in; the publishing certification with country statements; the email announcing a change; the sold or no-longer-available label, the seller's own list, the 12-month copy and timed erasure; in-app messages and blocking; the appeal of a rejection and review by a person on request; the steps of enforcement (warning, lower ranking, restriction, ban), to be confirmed against the moderation build; converted prices and automatic translation of ads; promoted ads; help from staff inside an account; copy, export and deletion of a user's data on request; the security check at sign-up. Also not built: the Report button on every ad and the posting of ads on ethio.com's own social channels. Left out of the drafts on purpose, because they are in the source ledger but not planned for launch: storefronts and verified badges (clause A9) and consent for conversations stored in another country (clause B5).

THE LAWYER'S LIST (six points; they wait for the Ethiopia-entity milestone and do not block launch): sending data from the EU or the UK to the United States needs a recognised legal mechanism and the draft names none; which countries the sanctions line in Terms 2 closes to users; whether the liability limit and the exclusion of warranties in Terms 12 must be printed more prominently to hold up in a United States court; whether the California privacy law applies (it has size thresholds ethio.com is unlikely to meet at launch); whether the copyright agent's phone number must also be printed on the site; how far the right in Terms 5 to act without notice holds for users in the EU and the UK. Also parked for counsel: an arbitration clause; whether a self-declaration of age meets Ethiopia's and Kenya's wording; the UAE child digital safety law; Ethiopia's one-year traffic-log rule.

RECORD LINES OWED IN PART H OF BUNDLE 4 (turn 10; none has landed): in `docs/spec/tos-privacy-source.md` — A13 amended (DEC-128), new A15 (the certification) and A16 (versions, notice, translation, settings), B9 with the final retention periods including messages and technical records, B13 (children), a correction of B2 ("precise GPS not collected" is wrong: the map-pin tool reads the device's position on "Use my location"), and the clauses adopted from the 2020 documents; in `docs/governance/launch-gate.md` — the pages and the acceptance live, the change email live before the first material change, the UK children's access assessment within three months of opening the UK, the UAE re-check, the legal@ and privacy@ mailboxes, the copyright agent's registration (the checklist already requires a native speaker's read of all Amharic copy, which covers the Amharic legal text); in the roadmap — L1 (the legal section), L2 (retention with the My ads screens), the Report button, the Your data page, the social-channel posting; in REQ-028 — gambling, betting and lotteries on the banned list.

Findings by the count: 69 of 231 posts print an amount with its unit, 21 an amount with no unit and 141 none; of the 69, 42 print several prices; Ethiopian sellers do not count plates — they sell a set "for N people", a whole pot, a weight, a piece or a fixed bundle; delivery is stated in 68 posts and serving at the event in 33; 54 posts say how early to order, most often 1 to 3 days where a number is printed. Plan: catering stays a service in Events & Catering; one new leaf, Food & Beverages › Cooked Food to Order (Amharic «በትዕዛዝ የሚዘጋጅ ምግብ», slug `cooked-food-to-order`), also shown under Services, takes cooked food sold by the package, pot, tray or kilo; Events & Catering holds 84 of the posts and the new leaf 93. Five questions: placement; several prices; new price answers; kitfo; halal and gluten-free statements.
- Supervisor's review (2026-10-05 00:02 UTC). Checks passed: the sums; every proposed help text within 240 characters; no answer with more than 5 aliases; no garbled Ethiopic; the note's claims about the catalogue match the batch 19 state. Two corrections from the code as bundle 4 built it. DEC-131 (2026-10-04, operator "AGREE ON ALL THREE") — A PRICE TABLE IS A LATER FORM FEATURE. One ad carries one price today. Sellers of occasion food print a ladder of sizes (12, 20 and 30 people, two grades) in one post: 42 of the 69 priced posts of the C28 catering tally print several prices. The operator accepted the curator's request for a price table on the price page — up to six lines, each a short label or "serves N" with an amount, lists showing "from" the lowest line — as a feature to build after the legal pages (L1) and My ads. Until it lands the rule is one ad per size, with the size typed into the title. Rationale: a menu of different dishes is not one ad; a ladder of sizes for one offer is. Status (2026-10-05): decided, not specified, not built; queued after L1 and My ads.

CLASS RULES FORGED IN THIS BLOCK (issued to the executor and the curator in rulings; none installed in the supervisor instructions or the executor's Knowledge in this period):
- The verification of a bundle turn checks every step of the brief against the diff, not the report's list of what was done (INC-432, S86).
- A function reachable from the app's connection never issues a DELETE or UPDATE without a WHERE clause (pg-safeupdate on the API roles); a DO-block proof does not exercise that connection — only a route test does (INC-432).
- A migration's mark is chosen by the rule AND above the ledger's current newest mark; a read-back asks for the specific mark, never `max(version)` (INC-433).
- A shape the door accepts is a shape every screen reads; a sentence in the docs is checked against the form before a curator is told to rely on it (INC-434, S90).
- A file row for a row that already exists carries that row's stored cells read at build time, never a constant; a test asserts its own rows' planned actions, not a shared roster's totals (INC-437, G28's sibling).
- A total asserted against database truth is read in the same poll step as the page, never from a snapshot (INC-334).
- A prompt that carries Amharic is built by script from a vetted file and delivered as a file; it is never retyped inline (S88).
- The executor reads CI at the raw addresses of the `ci-evidence` files, never by `git fetch origin ci-evidence` and never from `origin/main` (S87).
- A prompt's stop for a migration can only follow the prod apply, because the executor's tool applies at save; staging is the operator's hand (S91).
- A walk line is written from the form's behaviour, located in the code, never from the catalogue file (S92; the second occurrence after INC-422).
- Text the operator can hand over in one step (a live Knowledge file, an export) is asked for, not searched for (S89).

THE SECURITY SCANNING PROGRAM (decided by the operator 16:54Z after a plan written in another thread, `claude/security-scanning-plan-2026-10-05.md`).

DEC-132 (2026-10-05, operator: "Security plan — decided (operator, 2026-10-05): layers A, B and C … now; layer D's paid question at launch, default = the open-source scanners") — THE SECURITY SCANNING PROGRAM: GITHUB SWITCHES, CI SCANNERS, A WEEKLY REVIEWER; RUNTIME AT LAUNCH. Four layers. A — GitHub-native, free while the repository is public: Dependabot alerts (alerts only, never Dependabot pull requests), CodeQL default setup, secret scanning, private vulnerability reporting — switched on by the operator on 2026-10-05; push protection stays OFF (the executor's platform pushes commits itself and cannot answer a block) until secret scanning has run for a few days with no alert on the publishable values the committed `.env` carries (INC-000) — the supervisor's call, check-in 2026-10-09. B — scanners in our own CI, free and surviving the private switch (a G22 change, one executor bundle): a Semgrep CE job (rules from a pinned clone, no registry fetch at CI time; enforcing on ERROR; WARNING counts printed; SARIF to code scanning while public; a planted-finding fixture proves the job fails on bad input; the Semgrep version pinned — INC-009); the Supabase advisor lints (the open-source splinter rules) in the nightly against ethio-staging after the executor's census of the reachable path (G25), counts and classes only; `SECURITY.md` at the root (private reporting through GitHub's form; no bounty; the response time an admin setting per G48); an OWASP ZAP baseline job written but DISABLED (workflow_dispatch only) until DEC-116's private switch — until then a monthly docker one-liner the operator runs on his own computer, output kept with him. C — the weekly Claude security reviewer, a scheduled task in a fresh session, read-only (never writes to the repository, never messages an agent, never changes a setting, never prints a secret value): a diff review of the window (migrations, routes, dependencies, tracked files), Semgrep and gitleaks run locally, the ci-evidence guards and flake ledger read, a dated report; "nothing found" is written as a heartbeat line; every finding carries severity, file:line or table, class and proposed INC text; the live supervisor thread assigns INC numbers and turns findings into executor turns. D — runtime, at launch: Cloudflare (Bot Fight Mode, the free managed WAF ruleset, a rate rule on the auth and posting paths, Security Events read weekly by the layer C run on a read-only token), Supabase auth rate limits and logs, the app's own security heartbeats (REQ-032 family; DEC-091's admin), a paid penetration test weighed at the Ethiopia-entity milestone. Rules, pre-committed: (1) what blocks CI — a Semgrep ERROR; the dependency audit on high/critical (as today); a splinter lint of class ERROR (RLS disabled on a public table; a SECURITY DEFINER view or a function exposed to anon) not named in `scripts/public-surface-allowlist.txt`; WARN-class lints report, never block; a finding is an INC the same day; a Dependabot alert of high or critical becomes an executor task in the next brief. (2) What stays private — finding bodies never enter the public ci-evidence files or Actions logs, counts only; bodies live in the Security tab, the operator's own ZAP output or the reviewer's report; and, by G51, the detail of an unfixed hole never enters the public repository before its fix is live — the ledgers carry number, class, severity and status until then. (3) The reviewer's report is dated; a scanner that could not run is reported as not run, never as zero findings. (4) The private switch at launch (DEC-116) is made only after the repository is attached to the supervisor's session and a read is tested (G51); on that day CodeQL and secret scanning lapse unless the paid add-ons are bought (GitHub Code Security $30 and Secret Protection $19 per active committer per month — ESTIMATE $49–98 a month), weighed at launch against layer B, default layer B (G52); the launch gate carries the item. (5) The program is reviewed at the launch gate and whenever a scanner's rule set or version changes (G42). (6) No cost rises before launch: every layer A–C item is $0. Deviation (G10, 17:10Z): OSV-Scanner is dropped from layer C — its online service and its offline database host answered 403 from the reviewer's workspace; the dependency signal is CI's dependency-audit job plus Dependabot. Status: A ON (operator's report; push protection OFF by rule). B QUEUED as bundle 6, after this close. C LIVE: the scheduled task "Weekly security review — ethio.com", Mondays 06:41 America/New_York, next 2026-10-12; its baseline run of 16:43Z completed and its report lives in the Project (`claude/security-review-2026-10-05.md`); its prompt corrected at 17:09Z (gitleaks by pinned release URL v8.30.0; the history scan as a commit range; OSV dropped; Semgrep i18next-key-format results set aside and counted; NEW vs CARRIED findings; the alert feeds read only "if the API answers"). D at launch.

THE BASELINE REVIEW (window `7298f18b..d08c76cf`, 1,064 commits, 29 migrations, 31 route and server files; Semgrep 1.179.0 and gitleaks 8.30.0 ran, history 914 commits 0 leaks; OSV did not run). THE UNIT_AM QUESTION (18:0xZ). Asked by the operator: when Oromo or Arabic are added, do the six M8c functions "only work for Amharic"? Answer from the code: they carry one extra cell, the Amharic twin of `unit`, as every other catalogue cell does (`label_am`, `help_text_am`); nothing in them breaks for a third language, and nothing in them serves one either — a third language's catalogue words would come through `entity_translations` (the resolver tier DB[lang] ▸ `name_am` ▸ `name_en`, `src/i18n/entity.ts:8`) once attribute cells are given rows there. Q-004 stands (launch EN + AM, others fast-follow); the generalisation of attribute cells to `entity_translations` is a roadmap item, not a defect.

THE GITHUB RUNNER OUTAGE (19:23Z → 21:56Z). Run 37363017708 on `ba9a4530`, attempt 1: seven jobs never received a runner — GitHub's own annotation on each, "The job was not acquired by Runner of type hosted even after multiple attempts" (no steps, no runner name, cancelled by GitHub after 15 minutes; `ci.yml` sets no `timeout-minutes`); the E2E chain was skipped behind the cancelled build and the merge job reported "0/8 sources". Every job that got a runner was green. GitHub's status page carried "Incident with Actions" from 19:11Z. Classified PLATFORM-ORIGIN, no code finding, no INC; the operator's "Re-run failed jobs" was the remedy (attempt 2 cancelled by him at 21:20Z; attempt 3 drained from 21:29Z and completed SUCCESS 21:56:02Z, 25 jobs, 0 flaky, main promoted). The operator asked, while the queue stood, to have Lovable "check and fix the issue"; answered: not the code's to fix, and an executor push would have cancelled the run. The CodeQL default-setup run of the same minute lost its "Analyze (actions)" job the same way (re-run by the next push). Standing rule for the platform's own config package only (the seventh occurrence makes it a class): a bump the platform commits at a turn start is named in the first lines of the executor's report ("platform bumped <package> <a> → <b> at <commit>"), is judged by this rule on that turn's final commit, and needs no turn of its own — the executor cannot separate what it did not do; every other package or framework change keeps G42 whole.

DEC-135 (2026-10-05, supervisor; built in bundle 6) — A DRAFT SAVE THAT NEVER ANSWERS IS A TRANSPORT FAILURE. The draft save (`src/features/posting/posting-service.ts:71–86`) is a plain `fetch` with no timeout; a stalled connection leaves the wizard's save state "saving" for as long as the tab lives (`use-draft.ts:296–345`), while a thrown transport error shows "unsaved" (`:350–363`). Rule: a save with no answer within 30 s (ESTIMATE; the measured p99 of the draft route on staging supersedes it when the executor pastes it) is aborted by the client and takes the same path as a thrown fetch — "unsaved", retried by the next edit; the server-side cost is nil (the aborted request is the h3 abort line of INC-439, non-gating). Proof: a unit test on the saver with a never-resolving fetch; PW-32 unchanged. Not a cause finding for INC-439, which stays open on the nightly watch.

CLASS RULES FORGED IN THIS BLOCK (issued in rulings and briefs; G41 lists them for the next instructions version):
- A new column in an imported file format is placed where the format's gate keeps every older file valid — last and optional (`optionalFrom`) — and the change is sized by the fixtures it reaches, never by the files it names (S96; G37, G16 addendum).
- A red-first clause is written only after the pre-fix path has been walked to the line that answers: when today's answer and the fixed answer share a shape, the test provokes the branch that differs (S97; G38).
- A platform-committed bump of the platform's own config package is reported in the first lines of the executor's report and judged by CI on the turn's final commit; it never gets a turn of its own, because the executor cannot separate what it did not do (DEC-134; G42 narrowed for that package only).
- A CI red whose jobs carry no steps and no runner is a platform event: read the job annotations and GitHub's status before any prompt; a re-run is the remedy and an executor push the one thing that makes it worse (the outage of 2026-10-05; §8 RED-BOARD READING gains the class).
- The detail of an unfixed security hole does not enter the public repository: the ledgers carry number, class, severity and status until the fix is live; the Project record holds the body (G51, DEC-132 rule 2).
- An operator-initiated re-run of a workflow run is a different attempt of the same run; the status file names the attempt, and the verdict is read from the attempt that completed.
- A finder walk line is predicted by running the finder model against the merged catalogue (index, scope filter, matcher, ranking, the eight-row limit), never from the index sources alone (S95; G31).
- Before any categories create row goes to the curator, the open INC list for the importer is read (S94; G40, G47).
