DEC-136 (2026-10-06, supervisor under G17, listed as overridable in the bundle 6 brief, which the operator's paste approved) — A "REVISE" DIAL ON THE OWNER DOORS THAT WRITE A REVISION ROW. A new dial `revise`, 120 calls per hour per account, and `rate_gate('revise')` in six doors: `edit_listing`, `transition_listing`, `renew_listing`, `set_listing_pin` (the four the security review named, INC-444) and, by the class census, `mark_sold` and `relist_listing`. Not the `draft` dial (600 per hour): the purpose is a bound on the growth of `listing_revisions`. The refusal is `rateLimited` in each door's own style; the service's own calls are never counted; a refused call rolls its count back, so the gate counts calls that complete. Built in M9a (the dial row) and M9b (the six doors redeclared whole from the live definitions, one gate line each); tests PR-27 to PR-32. Watch item for the review desk's plan: the gate also counts a reviewer's or enforcer's transitions.

DEC-137 to DEC-147 — WHAT EACH IS AND WHERE IT STANDS AT THE CLOSE (their full texts follow this paragraph). DEC-137 — every CI job gates promotion unless a SIGNAL_ONLY list names it with its decision; a guard script with a bad fixture enforces it (KEPT: CI green on its landing, every job, main promoted). DEC-138 — a service-client read built from a list that can grow carries at most 4,000 characters of joined values per request; the teardown names the calls that ran out (RULE MET: "ran out: 0" on every run since). DEC-139 — a card-1 identity answered for the first time restarts nothing (built; ABSORBED by DEC-144). DEC-140 — a workflow file is linted before it is pushed, and in CI (KEPT). DEC-141 — the lint runs with its shell and Python checks off (KEPT: green on valid files and refusing its fixture over three runs — `a284bc55`, `48592859`, `0f5d2f82`; on `d766520f` the step's download failed before the lint ran — INC-468). DEC-142 — the maintenance block reaps scratch attribute definitions; `E2E_MAINTENANCE=1` lets a local run own maintenance (KEPT: the count of real definitions unmoved; 10 young scratch rows after the last run, under the 300 of the rule). DEC-143 — the failure report prints a result's further errors (KEPT). DEC-144 — OPERATOR: a changed answer resets only what depends on it; the parent is asked first; an earlier answer narrows a later one — the whole-form restart is retired (build: bundle 7 Part 1). DEC-145 — OPERATOR: screening is automatic from the first ad, with a stronger second layer; messages are screened; people are the exception (the screening spec; stage 2). DEC-146 — the E2E setup proves the app under test points at staging (KEPT on the CI lanes; the nightly and guard-proof lanes are judged at their next run). DEC-147 — the workflow-lint step's download retries five times, five seconds apart (rule: kept while the step is green on valid files over the next three runs and still refuses its fixture).

DEC-137 (2026-10-06; supervisor under G22; rule frozen before the change) — EVERY CI JOB GATES PROMOTION UNLESS NAMED SIGNAL-ONLY. `promote.needs` names every job of ci.yml except `promote` itself and the jobs a SIGNAL_ONLY list inside scripts/check-promote-needs.sh names with their decision (`e2e-changed` — DEC-023-B). The script fails on a job in neither list and on a needs entry that names no job; its self-test fixture must make it fail before the real file is judged. Evidence that the change altered nothing else: CI green on the landing commit, every job, main promoted. A red on that commit in one of the three newly gating jobs is a real red, fixed first — never answered by taking the job back out.

DEC-138 (2026-10-06; supervisor under G22/DEC-104; rule frozen before the change) — SERVICE READS ARE BATCHED BY LENGTH; THE TRANSPORT SUMMARY NAMES WHAT RAN OUT. A service-client GET built from a list that can grow carries at most 4,000 characters of joined values per request (helper `chunkByLength`, unit-tested); global teardown's DEC-104 line names the path of every exhausted call (path only — no query, header, key or body); the EN heal's log line says "probe complete" or "probe INCOMPLETE: <message>". No retry count, wait or timeout changes. Rule: shard 1's teardown reads "ran out: 0" on the next full CI run; if not, the printed paths name the rest and it is fixed in the turn it is read.

DEC-139 (2026-10-06; supervisor, inside D46/D47; overridable by the operator) — THE WHOLE-FORM RESTART NEEDS A PREVIOUS ANSWER. A root (the card-1 identity) answered for the FIRST time — its previous answer was empty — is handled as D25 handles any parent: only the details its options speak about are re-derived; nothing else the seller filled is touched and no Undo offer appears unless something changed. A change from one answer to another keeps D46/D47 exactly (PW-59, PW-60, PW-32, PW-61 unchanged). OPEN QUESTION put to the operator (it would amend his D46 ruling, so it is his): on the fifteen "Other …" leaves the card-1 question is a generic classifier (Condition, Homemade or Factory-made, Gender, Room, Sport, Provider type, Offer type…), and CHANGING it later still restarts the whole form, "What is it?" included (Undo for ten seconds). Recommendation: the restart applies only where the identity drives a dependent list (brand → model); a generic classifier restarts nothing (D25's narrow scope). Not in the v3 brief; built only on his yes.

DEC-140 (2026-10-06; supervisor under G22; rule frozen before the change) — A WORKFLOW FILE IS LINTED BEFORE IT IS PUSHED. Every turn that edits a file under .github/workflows/ runs a workflow lint before the turn ends and pastes its output: actionlint, pinned (version and sha256 named), if the executor's sandbox can run it (census first, G25) — then also one step in build-and-check, self-tested on a bad fixture (a `run` step carrying `with`); if it cannot, a structural check (every step exactly one of uses/run; `with` only beside `uses`) is pasted instead and no CI step is added. No package is added to package.json. Rule: the lint must report X1's error before the repair and nothing after; CI's own start on the next commit (a run named "CI" with build-and-check green) is the proof. Not solved by this DEC: a file GitHub rejects still leaves no evidence — DEC-096 (the stranding detector, decided, not built) is where "no run for dev's head" belongs.

DEC-141 (2026-10-06; supervisor under G22; rule frozen before the change) — ACTIONLINT RUNS WITH ITS SHELL AND PYTHON CHECKS OFF. `actionlint -shellcheck= -pyflakes=` in CI and before every push that edits a workflow file. Reason: the step's subject is the workflow file's shape (INC-457); shellcheck's version is whatever the machine carries. The two scripts shellcheck named are not edited. Rule: kept while the step is green on valid files and refuses the fixture for its own reason over the next three runs; if it reds a valid file again the step is removed and the pre-push lint (DEC-140) stays. Shell linting, if wanted, is its own decision with its own pinned tool.

DEC-142 (2026-10-06; supervisor under G22; rule frozen before the change) — THE MAINTENANCE BLOCK REAPS SCRATCH ATTRIBUTE DEFINITIONS; E2E_MAINTENANCE=1 LETS A LOCAL RUN OWN MAINTENANCE. Rule: kept while (a) the count of definitions without a scratch prefix is unchanged by every run of the reaper (proved at landing, re-read at the next census) and (b) the scratch count on staging after a CI run stays under 300; reverted, with an INC and a restore from the catalogue export, the day a real row is deleted.

DEC-143 (2026-10-06; supervisor under G22; rule frozen before the change) — THE FAILURE REPORT PRINTS A RESULT'S FURTHER ERRORS. Rule: adopted when the reporter's self-test passes with the CAP-2 needle; kept while every run's evidence file is written and the next timed-out test's body names its waiting action; reverted if the evidence file fails to be written or a body passes the size the reporter caps.

DEC-144 (2026-10-06; OPERATOR ruling; build = bundle 7 Part 1, to be explained before the brief) — A CHANGED ANSWER RESETS ONLY WHAT DEPENDS ON IT; THE PARENT IS ASKED FIRST; AN EARLIER ANSWER NARROWS A LATER ONE. 1. NO WHOLE-FORM RESTART. Changing any answer on the specifications step — the leaf's identity (card 1) included — re-derives only the questions that depend on the changed one (its children by option parent / depends_on, what its options fill or bound by facts, what its options allow, what its answer shows or hides by condition), and what those in turn decide. Everything else the seller filled stays. This RETIRES the whole-form restart of D25b ("a make change starts over") and D46 ("the identity starts the form over"); D47 falls with them; DEC-139 (a first answer restarts nothing) is absorbed — first answer or change, one rule. The ten-second Undo offer stays for whatever a change emptied. D59 (a CATEGORY change resets the category-shaped answers, with Undo) is unchanged (operator, 2026-10-02: "a main-category change resetting the form"). 2. PARENT FIRST. A question is never asked before a question it depends on. Enforced where rows enter (the importer and the console's order door refuse it — design in bundle 7) and written into the curator's limits. 3. NARROW, NEVER RESET UPWARD. Where an earlier answer makes a later choice or question not apply, the later one loses that choice or is not asked (allowed, condition); a later answer never rewrites an earlier one. A fact carried by a lower question about a higher one is a defect of the catalogue.

DEC-145 (2026-10-06; OPERATOR directive; reaffirms and amends the moderation design of record) — SCREENING IS AUTOMATIC FROM THE FIRST AD; A STRONGER SECOND LAYER; MESSAGES SCREENED; PEOPLE AS THE EXCEPTION. His words: "what i really want this site is to be fully automatic with ai reviewing and instantly approving all ads, screening add messages as well as images for appropriateness, etc etc just like what a human does and restricting add from going live and even restricting the person. as planned initially it should be minimal human involvement and we may nee dto have different levels of AI screening these adds say if the first is not sure or feels add needs to be blocked, the second layer that is more advanced model sees it and decides, etc. we should also moderate conversations as well between seller and buyer for appropriateness, etc. minimal human involvement is the plan. So stage 4 needs to come earlier." and on decision 1: "as discussed before, ai first from get go and train as wel go." What it reaffirms (docs/governance/moderation-design.md, 2026-09-17: one multimodal judge per publish and edit; three bands; "a second opinion before a person"; 24-hour holds that take the second pass's lean; the automatic ladder; automated first-round appeals; reports re-judged automatically; the weekly 50-decision sample as the only routine human task; §9 "what we deliberately do not build: … a human-first queue"). What it amends, for the screening spec: (1) the second pass is a MORE CAPABLE model, not only a different seed; (2) a first-pass NOK also goes to the second layer before the seller is refused (the design sent only UNCERTAIN there); (3) messages between buyer and seller are screened for appropriateness by the same pipeline (REQ-026 screened a new account's first message); (4) build order: the screening gateway directly after the legal section, before the ad page and My ads.

DEC-146 (2026-10-06; supervisor under G22; rule frozen before the change) — THE SETUP PROVES THE APP UNDER TEST POINTS AT STAGING. Before any browser test runs, `global-setup` checks through a pure helper (`e2e/helpers/app-target.ts`, `assertAppTargetsStaging`) that `VITE_SUPABASE_URL` carries the staging ref and that `SUPABASE_URL`, when set, does too; otherwise it stops with a message naming `bun run e2e:local`. KEEP when: (a) the unit test and a plain launch with `E2E_BASE_URL` set (so no server starts and nothing touches ethio-prod) stop in setup with the message and zero tests; (b) `bun run e2e:local` on the smoke spec is green with the log line; (c) CI on the turn's final commit is green in every lane. REVERT (forward only) when a lane that passed before fails on the check for a reason other than a non-staging target. The nightly and guard-proof lanes are judged at their next run by the same rule.

DEC-147 (2026-10-06; supervisor under G22, a tooling call; rule frozen before the change) — THE WORKFLOW-LINT DOWNLOAD RETRIES. The step "Workflow lint (actionlint, pinned)" fetches its pinned tool with `curl … --retry 5 --retry-delay 5 --retry-all-errors`; the sha256 check, the fixture self-test and the flags are unchanged. Reason: on run 37448727855 the release host answered HTTP 500 once and the build job went red before typecheck, lint and build ran (INC-468). KEEP while the step is green on valid files over the next three runs and still refuses its fixture; REVERT (forward only) if the step fails on a run where the release host answered. The line was run by the supervisor before it went into the ruling: the download succeeds and its sha256 equals the pinned value.

D-RULINGS OF THE PERIOD (operator, 2026-10-06, on the curator's `{country}` plan). D73 — Amharic takes no prefix before the `{country}` token (the names the token draws can themselves begin with the possessive letter); this replaces his prefix form of 2026-10-01. D74 — "Ships Nationwide" becomes "Ships Within {country}". D75 — the two "Made in" questions (`product_origin`, `origin-food`) stay exactly as they are until the form can offer an answer in some markets and not in others, so that "Ethiopian-made" is not lost outside Ethiopia; that form change is a posting-form extra. D76 — the tick-box "IMEI Registered (Works on Ethiopian Networks)" on phones and tablets is replaced by a market-neutral question, "Unlocked (works with any SIM card)" — after research he asked for: handset registration in Ethiopia was dropped on 23 August 2018 by the telecom operator's own announcement and no reliable source describes a rule in force today, while peers ask about registration only where it is enforced and about the network lock elsewhere. It is built as a NEW question, not a relabel ("registered" is not "unlocked", and a stored yes must not change its meaning). The production read-back of turn 10 found one draft holding the old answer, and the form sends a stored answer whose question is no longer linked — the door then refuses the save — so the old question is unlinked only after INC-467 is fixed in bundle 7; the curator proposes the Amharic and the help texts meanwhile, and the operator reads them before any row.

CLASS RULES FORGED IN THIS BLOCK (in force from the day adopted; issued in rulings, briefs and curator messages; carried to the next instructions version — G46).
- A curator audit EXECUTES every engine rule it cites against the rows — the effect on the screens, resolved as the engine resolves it — never only the cell's form; a simulation the curator reports is re-run by the supervisor's script (S98, S99).
- A statement about what the catalogue holds is made from RESOLVED rows: effective rows are resolved from the direct rows, nearest row first, and the resolver must first reproduce the export it was given, row for row (S107 — the fourth occurrence of the class; promoted to tooling).
- The audit reports every leaf where a question is ordered above the card-1 identity, and every question that depends on a question asked after it (S102; DEC-144 rule 2).
- The post-test transport line of the evidence file is read on every verification; "ran out" above 0 is a finding the same turn (S100).
- Every CI read names the server-error census's off-allowlist messages with their counts and the change from the run before (S110).
- A paged read is reviewed for a second writer; a count of leftovers in the thousands is fixed in the turn it is found (S106).
- A tool added to CI is read for what it calls out to, and its step is run with those integrations named on or off (S105).
- The local-run list of a brief is built from which spec files exercise the changed screen; an owed census is a condition of the turn's report, not a line in it (S104).
- A step that edits a shell command names the construct and is tried in a shell first (S103); a brief sentence that cites a settled decision is checked against that decision before it is sent (S101).
- A door's range is read before a client value is aimed at it; a console door's function is read before its use is told to an agent (S110, S111).
- Every stage plan and every proposal opens with a "checked against" list — the decisions, requirements and design documents it touches, each read at its file and line (S108).
- Every new order of work is cross-checked against every open list before it reaches the operator; an item without a place stops the plan (S109; the operator's directive).
- A question of fact is put to the operator on its own, never above a yes/no recommendation; an answer that can be read two ways is resolved before it enters a message for an agent; a fact he may not hold is researched, not asked (S112).
- Playwright is started only with `bun run e2e:local` or `bun run e2e:changed`; the setup refuses anything else that does not point at staging (DEC-146).
- A workflow file is linted before it is pushed, with the command CI runs (DEC-140, DEC-141).
- A catalogue plan never relies on the console's Merge, and sets no answer inactive, until INC-466 and INC-467 are fixed.

DEC-148 (2026-10-06; supervisor under G22 and DEC-132 layer B option ii; the rule frozen before the numbers existed) — THE DATABASE LINTS ARE FIVE COUNTS AGAINST A BASELINE. `public.security_lints()` (service role only) returns name, level and count for: rls_disabled_in_public (ERROR), security_definer_view (ERROR), function_executable_by_anon (ERROR), function_search_path_mutable (WARN), table_writable_by_client (WARN) — never an object name (DEC-132 rule 2). A nightly step reads them from ethio-staging and compares with `scripts/security-lints-baseline.json`: an ERROR-class count above its baseline fails the nightly; a WARN-class count above it prints one warning line; a count below it prints "baseline can be lowered"; the baseline changes only in a commit whose changelog line says why; a missing name is an error, never zero. Judged over the first seven nightly runs with the step: two failures for a reason other than a real count change (transport, a missing secret) → the step retries once on a transport error (DEC-104's class); it is not removed. The nightly stays outside promote. BUILT in M11 and turn 7 (`scripts/security-lints.ts`, its test, the step "Database security lints (counts only, DEC-148)"); baseline 0 / 0 / 27 / 0 / 0, the same counts on ethio-prod by the executor's read. AT THE CLOSE: one nightly run with the step, green (run 37582211006) — one of seven.

DEC-149 (2026-10-07 01:23Z; OPERATOR — "Agree" to the supervisor's three recommendations of 01:20Z, with one addition of his own; it changes the default of REQ-004; to be checked again in detail at the stage 2 spec, his standing rule) — AN AD'S FREE TEXT IS TRANSLATED AT POSTING; SCREENING IS FOR EVERY LANGUAGE AND NEVER SWITCHES OFF. His requirement: "say a user posts in a Oromifa, or Tigrigna. How do we know if this post passes our screening. so meaning that post definately needs to be translated. I dont think we can force a user to post in a language other than what the native language is, so when a user posts, edits etc we need to translate in all languages used by the website and display accordingly after screening ofcourse." (1) An ad's free text — title, description, write-in answers — is machine-translated into every site language when it is posted or edited, after the verdict; readers see their own language by default with "Translated automatically · See original"; the original is one tap away and governs. REQ-004's default was a "Translate" tap; REQ-025's index of every listing in every launch language is served by the same translations. (2) A seller correcting a translation of their own ad: later, not in the first build. (3) Chat messages: screened (DEC-145); translated only on a tap. (4) His addition: "we need to have a way to turn it off from admin side meaning keeping the screening but turning off translation incase we decide at some point the cost if too much. screening is very vital and that is a must for all languages anyway and we need to make sure that is definit." — translation has its own admin switch (D64's machine-translation switch), independent of screening; with it off, ads show as written and nothing is translated; screening has NO switch that lets an ad go live unscreened in any language: an ad in a language the judge cannot read with confidence goes to the second layer, never straight to live. DESIGN CONSEQUENCE for the spec: the verdict and the translation are separable in the judge's contract (one call when the switch is on; the same call without the translation fields when it is off), so the switch changes cost and nothing else. THE DESIGN PUT TO HIM AND AGREED IN OUTLINE (the stage 2 spec works it out): the judge reads the ORIGINAL text and the photos, never a translation alone; only free text is machine-translated (category, answers, units, price and place are drawn from the catalogue in every language); the ad goes live on the verdict and a late translation never holds it; a language is "proven" for screening only after a measured test set whose rule is frozen first; a deterministic check after translation (numbers, model codes, phone-like strings present; brands and places from a do-not-translate list); on an edit only the changed fields are translated again; the ad's text is data in the judge's prompt. COST — ESTIMATE (560 characters of free text per ad, four photos, a Flash-Lite-class model): screening and translation in one call about $2 per 1,000 ads with two site languages and $3.6 with four (band $1–6), against about $34 per 1,000 through a dedicated translation service; measured values supersede once the Services page has a week of numbers. OWED AT THE STAGE 2 SPEC: whether a title the form built from answers is stored as text; the index size for REQ-025; the Privacy text naming the provider; a language section in `docs/governance/moderation-design.md`.

DEC-150 (2026-10-07 01:33Z; OPERATOR — "agree. please make it part of our plan in the stages plan we have. Integrate it. agree with your recomendations."; it supersedes his answer 16 of 2026-10-06, "No star ratings at launch: yes", for the time AFTER opening; it builds on DEC-011's seam and replaces the research's change item C2; to be worked out in detail and checked against earlier decisions at the stage 8 spec) — REVIEWS AND RATINGS OF SELLERS. His request: "REVIEW AND RATING BY SIGNED IN USERS, AS WELL AS REVIEW AND REPLY SYSTEM BU USERS AND SELLER. This gives credibility rating to the poster as more people review the users post and give good rating, and on the other hand bad reviews may expose fake posts etc. … say a lodgged in user does bad reviewes of multiple posters, and we can flag that user for bad intentions and restrict and remove those posts as well". (1) Who may review: a signed-in user who CONTACTED that seller on the site — a tap on Show contact (`contact_reveals`) or an exchange of messages; no purchase is needed; one review per reviewer per seller; never one's own ads. (2) What: 1–5 stars with a short text; the review is tied to the ad it was about, the score to the seller; shown on the seller's card on every ad and on the seller page. (3) Every review and every reply is screened by the automatic judge before it shows (DEC-145) and translated like an ad (DEC-149). (4) Replies: the seller answers each review once, publicly; the seller can report a review and can never remove one. (5) A review that calls the seller a scammer starts an automatic check of that seller. (6) Reviewer abuse, both directions: many low ratings across many sellers, or an account that only praises one seller → a deeper automatic check; the reviews are hidden, the account loses the right to review, repeats restrict the account; a count alone never punishes. (7) Honest display: no average until five reviews; then the plain average with the count and the spread; the label "From signed-in users who contacted this seller. Purchases are not verified." — never "buyers" (a peer was charged by its regulator for labelling such opinions as buyers'; the EU rule that a site says whether and how it checks reviews). (8) One way at first: sellers do not rate buyers. (9) A `review` rate dial; an alert to the seller for a new review (needs stage 6's notifications). WHEN: with the seller page, stage 8, the first stage after opening; contacts are counted from opening day, so early users can review once it ships. SIZE: 6–10 executor turns, 2 migrations (ESTIMATE; band 5–12). NAMED AND NOT BUILT WITH IT: sellers rating buyers; an open reply thread; a review with no contact at all.

DEC-151 (2026-10-07; supervisor under G17 and G22, a guard's rule narrowed with its proof; frozen before the change) — THE MIGRATION CHECK LEARNS THREE THINGS. (1) The table rule sets aside a DO block in which CREATE TABLE and DROP TABLE occur the same number of times (at least once) and the text `e2e_mig_` occurs; a file with no other CREATE TABLE passes the rule and is PRINTED. (2) The real-row rule gets `scripts/migration-real-row-allowlist.txt` (file | statement head | reason; skipped and printed), with one line for M11's proof statement. (3) `MIGRATIONS_DIR` becomes overridable, so a draft is checked in a scratch folder before it is saved. Why: M11's proofs created and dropped a scratch table and updated a scratch row by its returned id — both sound, both refused by the text rules, and no later migration can clear a refusal that reads the landed file's text (S116). RULE: KEEP if the report's self-test lines show one pass and three flags for (1), one skipped-and-printed and one still refused for (2), and the Migration linter job is green on turn 7's commit; REVERT the script if any bad case passes. KEPT: the script exits 0 on dev in the supervisor's clone and the job is green in CI from `9a44e083` on.

DEC-152 (2026-10-07; supervisor under G17; precedent INC-201) — M12: THE CATEGORY IMPORT UNDO WALKS ITS BATCH IN A FIXED, STRUCTURAL ORDER. A helper `public.cat_import_undo_order(p_batch uuid) RETURNS TABLE(revision_id uuid, pos bigint)` (sql, STABLE, invoker; revoked from PUBLIC, anon and authenticated) numbers the batch's open rows: rows that are not creations first; creations ascending by the count of `cat_descendants` of their category (0 when it no longer stands) — every pointer, so a child always counts fewer than each parent; then `entity_key`; then `id`. `admin_undo_category_import` is redeclared whole with ONE statement changed (the loop joins the helper and orders by `pos`). Why a helper and not an inline clause: the door needs a permitted admin, so a proof block cannot call it; the helper lets the proof show the ORDER itself on scratch rows, and the route tests prove the door. Why not a recorded sequence column: it would not order batches written before it (and INC-480's class). Residual: none found — every order dependency of this undo is among creations. TESTS: CT-26 (the reproduction) and CT-42 (four rounds of a file that creates a chain with a guest link; a depth-only order would fail it), red first on the old function, green in CI on `549cfcfb`; 24 repeat passes.

THE OPERATOR'S QUESTIONS OF THE PERIOD, ANSWERED FROM THE CODE OR THE RECORD. New markets ("what happenes when we expand our service to kenya or somalia, or southafrica?"): the `{country}` token draws the country of the ad's own place and, before a place is chosen, the market the seller is browsing, so an ad placed in Kenya reads "Used in Kenya" with no catalogue change; what does not adapt by itself is listed (answers that exist in some countries only — D75; the Ethiopia-specific questions left as written by ruling; on an ad page the token must draw the AD'S country — a requirement of stage 3's spec); NEW LINE for the launch checklist: opening a market includes approving that country's name in Translations in every language the site carries. Amharic "hardcoding" ("HOW WILL THAT WORK WHEN WE HAVE OTHER LANGUAGES?"): `en.ts` and `am.ts` are the developer seed and the offline fallback; the live text of every language is the Translations console's approved rows; a third language needs no code. Where the bundle sits in the stages: answered as a short list. Whether the previously used categories were built: yes — Part D, live with the Publish. The Security tab's "Semgrep OSS is reporting errors" banner: it reports the whole CI workflow's last result, not the scanner's; it clears with the next green run. His reads at the close: the count of parent-first violations on ethio-prod is 0; Dependabot and secret scanning show no open alert.

CLASS RULES FORGED IN THIS BLOCK (in force from the day adopted; issued in rulings and briefs; carried to the next instructions version — G46).
- When a door gains a refusal, every caller of the door is read for the ORDER and the state in which it calls, and the brief names the test that walks that caller (S117; INC-481 — G29's database case).
- A release or tolerance rule at a door is verified with the sequence "save, save again unchanged" at the route, and with the form's own sender read (S114; INC-479 — the door-and-screen split of G35 applies to a release as to a refusal).
- A rule that DELETES answers acts only on a read that succeeded: a cached copy, an empty payload or a failed read is a typed absence, never an empty list (K4, K5; axiom 4).
- A migration's proof design is run past the migration check's text rules when the brief is written; "check before the apply" is asked only in a form the executor can perform — a draft in a scratch folder (S116; DEC-151).
- A helper a brief leans on is opened and its signature quoted (S115).
- A brief that names a later version of itself is delivered together with a scheduled check-in that writes it (S113).
- No migration proof writes a row in `auth.users` or any reserved schema: a signed-in caller is a random id in the claims; a proof that needs a user's rows is a browser test with leased accounts (the executor's stated limit, 2026-10-06).
- The stop report of a migration is itself the operator's cue to apply on staging and re-run the failed jobs; the supervisor reads the file whole the moment it sees the report and says only what it found. A ruling says "do not apply on staging until I say" for one class only: a migration that removes or rewrites existing rows (decided under G17 from his practice at M10 and M12; production already holds the migration when the report arrives).
- A red-first run is asked as "put the old file back, show the test failing, restore it and show an empty diff" — never as a named git command (the executor's sandbox refuses `git checkout`).
- A test that proves a fix must REACH the changed line: its input is checked against every gate in front of that line, and it waits for the settled state before it asserts (IB-3, twice vacuous; S46's class).
- A proof that can pass by not running is not a proof: no `test.skip` on a missing fixture — a failing expect (K2); a test never seen failing is shown failing on the old file before it is accepted (K3).
- A text a brief gives for a string is copied by key, character for character; the executor writes no Amharic of its own for a key the brief gives (X1; G43).
- An object indexed by a key from outside — a URL, storage, a cookie, a database row — is read through an own-property lookup (INC-485; `src/lib/own-key.ts`); a provider's string is data, never HTML (INC-483).
- A cleanup helper that matches nothing says so: a helper that takes keys refuses ids (INC-463's true cause).
- The Security tab's status banner follows the CI workflow's last result; an alert list is triaged whole before any fix is prompted, and ONE part carries every real one (G29).
- From D81 on, every new button or action label is two or three words.

WHAT BUNDLE 8 BUILT (three executor turns, the third in three sittings; 43 commits and 37 files from `205681a8` to `858b2137`). PART A (M14; INC-480, INC-482): `ALTER DEFAULT PRIVILEGES IN SCHEMA public REVOKE ALL ON TABLES | SEQUENCES | FUNCTIONS FROM anon, authenticated`, written without a FOR ROLE clause (it acts for the role that runs migrations); EXECUTE revoked from PUBLIC, anon and authenticated on fifteen caller's-rights functions that no migration had ever closed — three trigger functions (a trigger needs no EXECUTE to fire) and twelve helpers, the twelve granted to service_role (one of them is reached through a function a CHECK constraint calls: without the grant a service-role insert fails — modelled on a local Postgres before the brief was written); proofs in the file (each of the fifteen closed to both browser roles, the twelve open to service_role, no function outside extensions left executable through PUBLIC, a scratch table and sequence born with no privilege for either browser role). A new FUNCTION is still executable through PUBLIC at birth — that default cannot be revoked per schema — so `scripts/check-migrations.sh` gains the born-closed rule: every function created by a migration stamped after M13 carries, in the same file, a REVOKE that names the function and PUBLIC (`FUNCTION_REVOKE_FLOOR="20261007143529"`, three self-tests; `docs/features/ci-guards.md`, "Born-closed law"). `scripts/security-lints-baseline.json`: the third count 27 → 12. PART B (M13, and step B7 in M14; INC-477, INC-490): `public.validate_listing_attributes` folds an option's `allowed` list beside the bounds fold (only a parent answered with ONE value contributes; two contributions meet at their intersection; a list contributes nothing) and refuses a sibling's value outside the fold with the existing reason `optionNotAllowed`, unless the stored row already holds that same combination (the value among the prior's tokens and every contributing parent's one value unchanged) — eight of the 136 listings with answers held such a combination on the day; M14 adds one block directly after BEGIN that rebuilds the answers once (a string trimmed; an object's string `value` trimmed; a list's string elements and its objects' string `value` trimmed, order kept), so the bounds fold, the `allowed` fold, the show-when test and the judgement all read what will be stored. Route tests PR-41 (seven cases) and PR-42 (six cases), each shown red against the door before its migration. PART C: `src/features/auth/auth-error-key.ts` (pure; the mapper moved out of auth-service.ts) answers `auth.errorLeakedPassword` only when the provider's code is `weak_password` and its only reason is `pwned`; eight unit cases; one new string in both languages (the operator read the Amharic). PART D (D81; INC-488): thirty-two keys set and three removed by the vetted table — twenty-nine action labels shortened to two or three words, the three question-buttons of the sign-in page split into a text prompt and a button with an existing label; `scripts/list-action-labels.ts` with its unit test and `scripts/action-label-allowlist.txt` (one line) as the standing check (English three words, Amharic four; field and figure labels listed and never flagged); the seven-row Amharic import file for the live store. PART E (INC-487): PW-147 and PW-130 wait for the offered name built from both names before reading the list. PART F: docs (`attributes.md`, `translations.md`, `ci-guards.md`), three AGENTS.md lines (new functions born closed; the label check; the database tool is handed a migration's final text once), the roadmap's bundle 9 block.

DEC-155 (2026-10-07; supervisor under G17, listed to the operator as overridable — his paste of brief v1 approved it) — THE LEAKED-PASSWORD CHECK STAYS OFF ON ETHIO-STAGING; THE MESSAGE IS PROVEN BY UNIT CASES AND BY ONE LIVE WALK LINE. The provider's check adds an outside call to every sign-up and sign-in; the tests sign in thousands of times a run with random passwords (`Pw-` and 18 random bytes). The refusal's shape was read from the installed auth client (auth-js 2.110.9: code `weak_password`; reasons `length`, `characters`, `pwned`) and is pinned by unit cases on the mapper. The specific message shows only when "leaked" is the provider's ONLY reason; with any other reason the general line stands. On ethio-prod the check is ON (the operator's switch of 2026-10-07 10:33Z) and the message was seen there by him at the bundle's walk. REVISIT if a refusal is ever reported on the live site that the mapper did not name.

DEC-156 (2026-10-07; supervisor under G42 and G22, by DEC-134's rule; the operator told, not asked) — THE PLATFORM'S CONFIG-PACKAGE BUMP 2.25.2 → 2.25.3 STANDS. `@lovable.dev/vite-tanstack-config` moved in the platform's own commits at the start of turn 1 (the eighth such bump). READ, not ruled by its label: the two tarballs were fetched from the public npm registry and compared — the only change is the development-server module-preload plugin (`apply: "serve"`, behind `command === "serve" && isSandbox`); nothing in the production build path changed; the lockfile changes two lines; package.json stays exact-pinned. DEC-134's rule — the bump stands only if CI on the turn's last commit is fully green — is met (run 37626895782). CORRECTION TO DEC-134's NOTE "the package is private, with no public changelog": there is no changelog, but its tarballs are readable from the public registry (`npm pack @lovable.dev/vite-tanstack-config@<version>`), so every later bump is read by comparing the two tarballs before it is ruled.

DEC-157 (2026-10-07; supervisor, flagged to the operator in one line as overridable) — pg_trgm STAYS IN SCHEMA public. The platform linter's "Extension in Public" line is ruled, not left dangling (G13): the extension's 31 functions are pure text functions and hold no data; it is used by three indexes and by `catalog_find`; moving it means redeclaring the search function and risks the three indexes' operators for no closed hole. REVISIT at ops hardening (phase 9) or if a lint level changes.

DEC-158 (2026-10-07; supervisor under G42 and G22; the operator told in one line) — THE PLATFORM'S BUMP 2.25.3 → 2.26.0 STOOD PROVISIONALLY, ITS JUDGE THE PUBLISHED SITE; THE PLATFORM THEN SET THE PACKAGE BACK ITSELF. Read by comparing the two public tarballs: one functional line — inside the platform's own build environment the server bundle is minified by default (`nitroOpts.minify = process.env.LOVABLE_SERVER_MINIFY !== "0"`, under `isSandbox`). CI builds outside that environment, so green CI cannot judge the change. RULE, frozen on the day: at the close of the bundle the walk includes three server-backed actions on the published site (a sign-in, a draft saved through step 3, the admin console opening); any failure there that the unminified E2E build does not show ⇒ package.json and bun.lock are restored in their own commit, Publish, walk again. OUTCOME: at 22:0xZ the platform's own commits set the package BACK to 2.25.3 (the registry then listed 2.26.1 and 2.26.2); the published build of the close is on 2.25.3 — the version DEC-156 read. The rule was therefore not exercised; it stays on record and applies again, unchanged, the day a 2.26.x returns.

DEC-159 (2026-10-07; supervisor under G17; listed to the operator as overridable — his paste of brief v3 approved it) — `geo_distance_km` IS CLOSED TO THE BROWSER ROLES WITH THE OTHER ELEVEN HELPERS. Its migrations (`4e1e819a…` :240, `55ecfe24…` :34) granted it by name to anon and authenticated and a proof of the day asserted that anon holds EXECUTE (the L1a note "public"). DEC-160 (2026-10-07; supervisor under G17 and G22 — the harness is a closed surface, opened by this named decision; listed to the operator as overridable — his paste of the prep prompt approved it) — THE ENGLISH RESET RUNS IN EVERY TEST SETUP, BEFORE THAT RUN'S TESTS. Cause (INC-493): `e2e/global-setup.ts` reset ethio-staging's English rows of the text store to the compiled catalog only behind the maintenance gate — shard 1, or `E2E_MAINTENANCE=1` — so the other five shards, the smoke, email and fast lanes, the nightly and every local run started their tests against whatever the store held; after any change of an English label, a test that finds the label by its words failed until shard 1 had finished its setup. Shape as built in the prep turn (commit range `858b2137`..`40d99978`): the block moves in front of the gate; it reads the English rows one REST page at a time, by key, through the existing `readAllPages` (`src/lib/read-all-pages.ts`), compares them in memory through the pure `staleEnKeys` (`scripts/en-baseline-heal.ts`; five unit cases beside it) and writes only the rows that differ or are not approved; scratch keys and Amharic are never touched; every setup prints `[e2e:setup] healed <n> stale EN rows (INC-175; probe complete)`; the reaper, the prunes, the storage sweep and the tripwire keep their one owner (DEC-036). Chosen over "the preflight job resets" because one place then covers CI, the nightly and the executor's local run. RULE, frozen before any number existed: the change stands if over the next five CI runs no setup shows a statement timeout (57014) that the runs before it did not show and no lane's setup grows by more than five seconds; otherwise the reset moves to one owner that runs before every lane, under its own decision. HOW THE RULE CAN BE READ (stated at the first read, 2026-10-08 — G7): the supervisor's channels carry no setup log line and no setup timing; a setup that throws turns its lane red and is seen; an incomplete probe is caught and printed, and is NOT seen. The second measure is therefore read through the email lane's whole job (one test): 61 s, 46 s and 42 s on the three green runs before the change, 42 s on the re-run of `858b2137`, 56 s on the first run with the change — inside the earlier range, and a range wider than the five seconds the rule names. So the rule is judged as: no lane red in its setup over five runs, and the email job inside 40–65 s; and the executor's first code turn of bundle 9 owes the setup's duration with and without the block from one local run (F1.5 asked for it and the report did not carry it). PROOF STATUS, said plainly: the local run of F1.5 printed "healed 0" — the stale row had already been reset by the CI re-run — so the fix was not exercised end to end; the unit cases prove the comparison and the printed line proves the placement; the first English label that bundle 9 changes is the end-to-end proof (expected: no first-run red in the email lane or in R-4).

THE HOUSE STYLE AS AGREED (D83–D85; bundle 9; NOTHING BUILT). The supervisor first read the peers (G49): the four official Filament themes and the "Sharp" one, the tables' layout, actions and grouped-actions pages, filters, the mobile-preset and advanced-tables plugins. The proposal was drawn on a private design canvas in the app's own colours and fonts, in the end five boards with levers: a list page on a desktop; the same list on a phone (one card per row); the control sheet (buttons, the action icons with one colour each, the three-dots menu, switches, badges, fields, the confirm dialog, the empty list); the whole page on a desktop (top bar 56 px, the left menu with 36 px rows and a lever "Open | Icons only", panel tabs and breadcrumbs as one band that stays, the page at 16 px padding and full width); the whole page on a phone (top bar 52 px that stays, a 48 px icon strip that opens to the menu, a bottom bar, the page at 8 px padding). AGREED, as the roadmap's bundle 9 block words it: colour by meaning as tokens (primary, danger, success, warning, info, neutral), corners 6 px, stronger borders, fine row rules, soft shadows on cards, tables and figures and stronger ones on menus and dialogs; row actions everywhere as Edit (grey) and Delete (red) icons with a three-dots menu for the rest, every icon button with a name and a tooltip, one shared building block; tables with a toolbar (search, Filters with its count, columns), filter chips with "Clear all", a selection bar, and a footer holding the count at the left, rows per page in the centre and the page numbers at the right, one card per row on a phone; the shell — every bar stays in place on every screen size, the left menu has no scroll bar of its own on a desktop, becomes an icon strip on small screens that opens to the full menu, and holds Sign out at its foot, breadcrumbs stay; a bottom bar on phones; one page padding owned by the shell (8 px on phones, 16 px above) with list pages at full width; one written rule and one automatic check per element with a baseline that only shrinks; the public marketplace pages (cards in two columns) designed afterwards under the same rules. READ IN THE CODE FOR IT: the space he sees wasted is two paddings stacked (`app-shell.tsx`'s `main` and `PageShell`: 28 px a side on a phone, 40 px from md, 48 px from xl); on phones the header is in flow and scrolls away; the location row renders only on the marketplace feed; the console law C8 ("one edit icon per row") changes to edit and delete icons plus the three-dots; there are no tokens today for success, warning or info. NOT TAKEN from the peers and said to him: saved views, the filter builder, colour themes. STILL TO CONFIRM WITH HIM when bundle 9 is explained (he approved the look and did not name them): the bottom bar's five items (drawn: Home · Saved · Post · Messages · My ads — Saved arrives with stage 5 and Messages with stage 6, so the bar opens with three live items); 36 px menu rows are for mouse devices and touch keeps 44 px (Knowledge C2). The boards' words "Post ad" and "My ads" are the drawing's; the app's own words stand. WHERE IT LANDS (decided under G17 as a plan, in the roadmap since bundle 8's Part F): its own bundle, BUNDLE 9, after bundle 8 and before stage 1, so that stage 1's pages are born under it. ESTIMATE, labelled: tokens, shared blocks and checks one to two executor turns; the shell about two; moving the existing screens three to five — five to nine in all. THE CENSUS FOR IT (`docs/governance/briefs/bundle-9-census.md`, written by the executor in the prep turn): an index by search — counts and file:line for tokens, blocks, buttons (outline 101 uses; 112 plain `<button>`), row-action icons (24 places), tables (DataTable 13 uses, one hand-built table), badges (27), fields, the frame's files, corner, border and shadow classes, the tests and written laws that pin today's look (227 lines), the public pages and the constraints — WITHOUT the line-by-line detail the brief asked for; its own "Not read" section says so. Decided under G17: no second executor pass; the supervisor reads the code at the census's addresses itself while writing bundle 9's brief (G3).

THE TEXT STORE (INC-488, confirmed and closed for this bundle). The provider layers the compiled English, then the compiled language, then the database's approved rows; "Sync keys" upserts every English key to the compiled text and fills an Amharic row only while it is untranslated or empty. So a changed English text reaches the live site only after "Sync keys", and a changed Amharic text for an existing approved row only through the console's import (the row becomes `edited`) and "Approve all reviewed". Read on the published site in turn 1: five live texts differed from the code (two English, three Amharic); the live store held 579 keys per language against 2,070 in the code. CLASS RULES FORGED OR RESTATED IN THIS BLOCK (in force from the day adopted; carried to the next instructions version — G46).
- The stop report of a migration that says the production apply SUCCEEDED is itself the operator's cue: he applies the new migration to staging, presses "Re-run failed jobs" and brings the report; the supervisor reads the file in parallel. He does not apply when the report says the apply failed or ends in a question. Block S56 had already written this rule (with one exception class: a migration that removes or rewrites existing rows); briefs v2 and v3 and both rulings of turn 3 nevertheless carried "wait for my go" — S121 — and D86 is the operator's own statement of the rule.
- The database tool is handed a migration's FINAL text once: never a stub, a comment or a draft; a scratch draft does not outlive the turn (INC-491 at its third occurrence; in AGENTS.md since turn 3).
- A ruling or brief step never depends on a file in the executor's scratch space surviving a turn boundary; what must survive is in the repository (S119).
- An expectation about the DATABASE in a brief (a count, a set, a shape) is taken from live rows the executor pasted, not from a static reading of the migration files; where only a static reading exists the brief says so and tells the executor to shape the statement from the rows it reads (S118).
- A function that judges an answer reads that answer through ONE normalisation at its entry; a second reader of the raw input is a defect (INC-490 — "one function, two readings of the same input").
- Every bundle-close CI read includes the conclusion line of `nightly-status.md` (the nightly of 2026-10-07 was written at 09:39Z, was not looked at in the CI read of 09:58Z, and was read at 10:40Z).
- A platform package bump is read by comparing the two public tarballs before it is ruled (DEC-156); a change that only the platform's own build environment makes is judged on the published site, not by CI (DEC-158).
- A rule restated to an agent carries the term that decides its edge cases, quoted from the code that enforces it (S120; S82's family).
- A census that must read every file line by line is its own executor turn, or is the supervisor's own reading; it is not added to a turn that also changes code (S122). A proof asked of the executor must be able to FAIL on the day it is run (S122: F1.5 could not, once the re-run had reset the row).
- The close of a bundle that changes an existing string carries "Sync keys", the Amharic import and "Approve all reviewed" as numbered operator steps (INC-488).
- A setting the operator holds is read on his screen before it is described to him as on or off (restated from S56; the leaked-password switch).

(1) One brief file, replaced whole by a new version per part, each written while the turn before it runs (D87): Part A tokens; Part B the shared blocks; Part C the frame; Part D the pattern console (Admin › Categories), then — after the operator's first look — every other screen; Part E the rules and checks. THE FEED ENGINE (D99–D101; bundle 10; spec `docs/governance/feed-engine-spec.md`, imported by this records turn with its approval line). Today the feed reads every active listing with no order and no page and sorts in the browser (INC-505), a category's page reads only its own category (INC-503), a subcategory's address selects nothing (INC-504), and the place filter is not built. The approved design: one table `feed_index` with a row for every active listing under each of its category's ancestors and each of its place's ancestors (its coverage places included), ordered by tier, then newest, then id; written inside the write doors at publish, edit, expiry and every state change, re-built for a category move by a job with a heartbeat, checked against the listings every night; one function `feed_page` and one route `/api/feed` that read a page of 20 after a cursor (keyset paging, never an offset), with the catalogue's cache headers for visitors who are not signed in; the widening ladder city → region → country → world when a step has fewer than 8 listings. THE FIVE DECISIONS (D101): (1) the targets, frozen before building — at 100,000 scratch listings on staging every page shape answers in 50 ms or less at the 95th percentile, a page weighs 30 KB or less, a cached page's first byte comes within 100 ms, and the first page shows within 1.5 s after the shell on a slow 3G profile (indicative); (2) "nearer step first, newest within a step" orders the feed instead of an exact distance sort — REQ-005's order for the feed is AMENDED by this decision; (3) the widening threshold is 8; (4) 20 per page, the next page loaded as the last card comes into view; (5) the engine is built next, before bundle 9's second half. Three turns (ESTIMATE, the spec's §7): E1 `feed_index`, its refresh function, the backfill of today's active listings and the consistency check (Tier A, one migration); E2 the write doors call the refresh, the tree-change re-index job, `feed_page` and `/api/feed` with its cache (Tier A, a second migration and a new public read); E3 the feed on the route with cursor paging, subcategory addresses resolved through the whole tree, the breadcrumbs and the performance job (Tier B).

CLASS RULES FORGED OR RESTATED IN THIS BLOCK (in force from the day adopted; carried to the next instructions version — G46).
- The next file for an agent is prepared while that agent works (G32 forbids delivering it, not preparing it); a bundle's records sources are written while its last code turn runs and its carrier is built the moment that turn is verified; a check-in that finds nothing to report is used for that preparation (D87; S123).
- A file handed to the operator goes first and alone, as one action line; an explanation follows it, never wraps it (the 01:38Z note: a brief inside a long explanation was read as "to be discussed").
- The frame is chosen by the window's width only, never by the device or the browser; a width walk across the breakpoints guards that no width between two frames breaks (D88).
- A frame change that takes width from pages is checked against every minimum width a test pins (`toBeGreaterThanOrEqual` on a width at 360), not against sideways overflow alone (INC-499).
- A census of a moved or changed element searches its test id, its accessible name AND its string key (INC-500 widened by S130's first part).
- Before a brief changes a shared helper's behaviour at a width, every caller's surrounding lines — open and close pairs, width guards — are read, not only the call sites (S130's third part and S131: the second occurrence).
- A shell helper is locale-agnostic: it finds the frame's controls by test id, never by an English name (INC-121, restated by S130).
- A test that walks widths resizes one loaded page; it does not reload a signed-in page at every width, and a test is sized by the page loads it makes against its timeout and the request limits (S129; G37's family).
- A test of a shared roster asserts the rule, not one database's count (the strip's capacity, 07:01Z — G28's family).
- A colour check waits for transitions to settle, as a box measurement does (INC-498: INC-384's rule).
- A route guard on an auth path is changed only on a measurement read against a judge frozen before the numbers, with its redirect unchanged (C2e.2).
- While the executor cannot start a browser, CI on each turn's final commit is the browser proof, reported as "local browser runs unavailable", and nothing is changed in the project to work around it (INC-506).
- Flake ledger lines are counted by run and test, not by line, until INC-495 is fixed.
- A scratch key drawn at random for a unique column is retried on a collision or drawn from a space wide enough for every lane at once (INC-502; the census of such helpers is with its fix).
- A name cut with "…" keeps its first five characters (a shorter name stays whole) in a part that never shrinks, so the rule holds in every font and script; a test measures the visible characters, never a box width (D104; S132).
- The focus ring shows after keyboard use only; after a tap or click on a menu's item it does not stay on the button — at the shared menu and select primitives (decided under G17 at the re-check, overridable).

WHAT THE FIX TURNS BUILT.
- `CutText` (src/components/ui/cut-text.tsx) is one inline grid cell holding three elements:
  - the visible element holds the whole name, so the accessible name and textContent are the name, once;
  - an unpainted, `aria-hidden` element whose CSS-generated content (`.cut-floor::before`, the first five graphemes and "…") sets the smallest width;
  - an unpainted, `aria-hidden` element whose generated content (`.cut-full::before`, the whole name, breakable anywhere) sets the widest width.
  So a name never shows fewer than its first five characters, a name of five or fewer stays whole, and a row of cut names can shrink.
- `useLabelRoom` (src/components/ui/use-label-room.ts): when a row overflows, its optional parts step aside in order — the label first (`sr-only`, still read by screen readers), then the elements its caller names (the place boxes' arrows, `hidden`).
- The input marker (src/lib/input-modality.ts, installed once in RootComponent's effect) sets `html[data-input]` to pointer or keyboard. One CSS rule empties the ring variables on buttons, links and menu roles after pointer input. Text inputs keep their ring. Focus still returns to the control.
- Twenty user-facing cut names moved to CutText:
  - the shell: header, rail and menu, bottom bar, panel header and tabs, location row;
  - the listing card;

THE OPERATOR'S LOCATION QUESTION (00:45Z, his words in the record). He asked whether the location box fills from the IP address ("very vital and pillar of the build"), whether the location is saved for the next visit on the same device, and asked for a notice when the saved place differs from the IP. Read in code and answered at 00:55Z:
- the IP guess is built (DEC-063). On the Lovable address it gives the country only. Region and city come by geometry once ethio.com serves the new app through his Cloudflare zone, where the visitor-location headers are on;
- the chosen area is kept for a year in the browser (`ethio_area`) and wins over the guess. The guess is never saved. Nothing keeps the area on the account;
- no "different place" notice exists;
- the feed does not narrow by place yet (bundle 10).
Two decisions were put to him with recommendations, and he agreed both at the walk (02:33Z, his words: "agree with the two decisions, - amber color should match the themes we selected earlier with the new feel and look following the filament themes. proceed"):
- D106 — the chosen place is kept on the account for signed-in people, so it follows them to every device. Visitors keep the per-browser save. The IP guess is still never saved.
- D107 — when the saved place differs from where the IP says the person is, one line appears under the location row: "You seem to be in <place>. Show listings there?" with Switch and Keep. It uses the house style's amber, Part A's `warning` meaning tokens (the Filament-like look of D83–D85), never a raw colour. After Keep it stays quiet until the IP's place changes again. The place is never switched on its own.
Both are one turn after bundle 10's E3 (ACT-017), Tier A for the account part; its spec is written with E3's brief.

CLASS RULES FORGED IN THIS BLOCK (in force from the day adopted; carried to the next instructions version — G46).
- A shared block's markup that a brief dictates is tried by the supervisor in a browser (the workspace's Chromium) for its accessible name and its intrinsic sizes before the brief is sent (S133).
- A census of the tests that reach a changed element searches its test id, accessible name, string key AND class or tag selectors (INC-500's rule, widened a second time).
- A test about a visually hidden element names its mark (the `sr-only` class or a box of 1 px or less), never "visible" or "hidden" (S134).
- A "no ring" check reads the drawn rings (a shadow layer with a visible colour and a spread), never string equality.
- A brief never tells the executor to overwrite AGENTS.md. A change the executor makes there is reviewed and recorded; a change wanted there is asked as a question (the refusal of 01:18Z).
- A cut name is never split into separate boxes: the floor comes from a size-setter that carries no text, so the name stays one piece for screen readers (INC-510).
